<meta>This page serves as the concluding passage of the third book within the second section. The author is finalizing his defense of "essential philosophy," linking his intellectual labor directly to Christian mystical truth and preparing the reader for the end of this specific volume.</meta>

### SECOND SECTION. BOOK III. Member II.

<margin>See page 7.</margin>
Although the labor of my expression is great, my better efforts will appear not without fault or crime in the eyes of the curious, though perhaps they will be pleasing enough to others. Therefore, I think it suffices that I dare to be confident in the fundamental subject of this **essential Philosophy**. This is because it has truth itself to defend and vindicate it, without any superficial adornment of words. And hence, as **truth does not seek out corners** <note>original: "veritas non quaerit angulos," a traditional legal and philosophical proverb meaning that the truth is bold and has no need to hide in shadows or use deception</note>, it has no need for eloquent words, polished sentences, or phrases to illustrate it or make it clearer in the eyes of the wise and the learned. 

Since it can be hidden from no one that truth conquers all things: for it is a bright splendor or emanation, rising from the almighty and eternal source, which illuminates all things and reveals itself in its effects to everyone. Therefore, it is so manifest in all its works that it does not need any golden-tongued orator, any smooth or methodical rhetorician, or any eloquent philosopher to reveal its excellence or report the perfection of its joys to the whole world. Nevertheless, the unworthy people of this world do not wish to acknowledge or receive it with the reverence it deserves. Instead, they perpetually hide it with all their might behind a veil of dark ignorance. In doing so, they do not cease to daily persecute and crucify the **spiritual Christ**, who is the only truth, the true wisdom, the true **Cornerstone** <term>Cornerstone (Lapis Angularis): A biblical title for Christ, but in this context, it also refers to the "Philosopher's Stone," the central secret of the author's sacred science</term>, and the essential subject of true and legitimate Philosophy. 

Blessed be His name forever, He who alone built the heavens, the earth, and all things within them, and who sustains and preserves them by the life-giving power of His spirit. He works all things in all people, and reigns in glory and power with the Father for eternity. He will deign to defend His servants from the evil-hearted; He will act as a shield and a place of refuge to preserve the herald of His truth from the viperous tongues of the wicked, from backbiting mockers, and from the poisonous taunts of the cynical and satirical **Momus** <note>In Greek mythology, Momus was the god of mockery and unfair criticism; here, the author uses him to represent his intellectual enemies</note>. It is He who handed down these words to the cultivator of His truth:

<margin>Luke 21:15</margin>
> I will give you a mouth and wisdom, which all your adversaries will not be able to resist or contradict. 

And so, I shall conclude with the Prophet David:

<margin>Psalm 63:8</margin>
> In the shadow of your wings I will sing: Lord, in you I trust: Vindicate and preserve me from my enemies according to your mercy. Amen.

->THE END.<-

# A RESPONSE
->TO<-
## THE SPONGE FOR THE WEAPON-SALVE
->OF MASTER FOSTER, PRIEST, PREPARED BY HIM<-
->FOR THE PURPOSE OF DESTROYING THE VALIDITY<-
->OF THE WEAPON-SALVE.<-

->that is,<-

### THE SQUEEZING
->OR THE CRUSHING<-
### OF THE SPONGE
->OF MASTER FOSTER, PRIEST.<-

->In which<-

**The supposed power of that sponge in wiping away**
**the weapon-salve <note>original: "unguentum armarium," a medicinal ointment applied to the weapon that caused a wound rather than the wound itself, based on sympathetic magic</note> is squeezed out, crushed, and**
**utterly abolished; and finally, his immodesty and incivility**
**toward his fellow brothers is corrected and completely**
**extinguished by the sharpest vinegar of truth.**

> The sharpest bile, corrected by the sharpest vinegar, is rendered sweeter.

> The brutish and foolish man does not understand the works of God.
> ->Psalm 92:6 <note>original: "Psalm 92. 7," following the numbering of the Latin Vulgate</note><-

->BY THE AUTHOR,<-

## ROBERT FLUDD, also known as "of the Floods,"
->Esquire <term>Armigero: A person of the social rank of a gentleman, entitled to bear a coat of arms</term> and Doctor of Medicine of Oxford.<-

## TO THE LEARNED READER, GREETING.

**HOW** far from my mind that rash madness has always been—the desire to call anyone into question through words or writings without being summoned by them or unjustly provoked by injuries—I commend to your sounder judgments (judicious readers). For I acknowledge that I myself am not without errors in my writing; why, then, should I do to another what I would not want done to me? Hence, is it right that I should detect a speck in my brother’s eye, when in my own view a beam is clearly seen? <note>A reference to the Gospel of Matthew 7:3 regarding hypocrisy.</note> Or does it befit one nurtured in Philosophy to better illustrate or adorn his own works through the damage or errors of another (if there be any)? On the contrary, I ask you, what mortal would not strive to restore his fame and dignity—withering as they are under the aspersions of the envious, the malevolent, or the ignorant—using the balm of truth <note>original: "opobalsamo," an expensive, aromatic resin used here as a metaphor for healing truth.</note> for himself? We notice that even the tiny fly is endowed with its own spirit <note>original: "splene," literally 'spleen,' then thought to be the seat of anger or spirit.</note> and the little worm is armed with gall, being inclined by a certain natural instinct to repel the injuries of those who oppress them.

Certainly, the one who first attacked my writings—and that without any summons from me—and called into question my *Harmony of the World*, was that great professor of astronomy **Johannes Kepler**; a man, I confess, who was learned, and in his analytical inquiry not so rash and immoral as those who came after him. He indeed walked with a more modest foot and did not overstep the laws of the ancients. <note>original: "limitum Ethnicorum," likely referring to the established boundaries of classical or non-Christian learning.</note> Instead, in the manner of an experienced Philosopher, he argued his case for harmony against my own with sufficiently solid arguments. To him, as was proper, I gave a response so full that it satisfied not only the world but even the man himself (inasmuch as he was not at all addicted to vain quibbles). But then, indeed, **Marin Mersenne, the Monk** <note>Marin Mersenne (1588–1648) was a French mathematician and philosopher who famously engaged in a heated debate with Fludd.</note> rose up against me and my Doctrine. Insofar as he was likewise treating the harmony of the world in his own manner, he could not tolerate the honorable reputation of my *Harmony*. He not only lashed out at it and my works, but even exceeding the genuine limits of Philosophy, he did not blush to utter sharp insults against me that no person of sound mind should have to endure.

Finally, a certain minister or Presbyter <note>Referring to the English clergyman William Foster (1591–1643).</note>, flanked by a crowd of various malevolent people (though they call themselves "Learned" in their own fashion) and supported by them as if by a pillar, began to pursue my good name with hatred and envy. He rashly, and without a shadow of a doubt, names many learned men famous throughout all Europe—namely **Paracelsus**, **Oswald Croll**, **Giambattista della Porta**, **Gerolamo Cardano**, **Ernst Burgrav**, **Rudolph Goclenius**, **Jan Baptist van Helmont**, and finally me (the least of the Apostles)—as <term>Cacomagi</term> <note>From Greek: 'Evil Magicians.'</note>, poisoners, and superstitious people. He does this in a certain English treatise of his titled ***HOPLO CHRISMASPONGVS*** <note>Translated from Greek as 'The Sponge to Wipe Away the Weapon-Salve.' This was Foster's 1631 attack on the use of sympathetic ointments.</note>, because we attempted to prove that the **weapon salve** <note>original: "unguentum armarium," a medicinal ointment applied to the weapon that caused a wound rather than the wound itself, believed to heal via magnetic or sympathetic forces.</note> is a gift from God, and that its effects are purely natural and not Diabolical. And as for what concerns me, he proceeds further in his drunken manner: for everything I alleged in Book 2, Chapter 9, in defense of the weapon salve or **Magnetic medicine**, he treats like a <term>Glorious Thraso</term> <note>A 'Thraso' is a stock character from Roman comedy—a "braggart soldier" who boasts of deeds he did not do.</note>, although tastelessly...

<summary>In this preface, the author defends his character and his "Harmony of the World" against critics like Kepler and Mersenne, specifically addressing William Foster's accusations that his "weapon salve" medicine is demonic magic.</summary>

Fludd is explaining why he is publishing this defense in Latin after previously publishing a version in English.</meta>

-># EPISTOLA AD LECTORVM.<-
-># TO THE READER, GREETING.<-

<note>This text continues from the previous page where Fludd was describing the unprovoked attacks on his work.</note>

...sufficiently, he attempts to refute me. It was therefore my duty above all to show myself to my fellow countrymen to be entirely innocent of that matter about which he so sharply accused me (since his pamphlet was printed in the English language). And although I issued this response of mine to the public in the English language as well (as was appropriate), I have nevertheless set it out here, translated into Latin, for this reason: first, because it relates to the unfolding of the mysteries of sympathy and antipathy; and especially because I have published it—at the persuasion and encouragement of certain of my learned friends—to more clearly reveal and demonstrate to the world that <term>magnetic secret</term> <note>Fludd refers here to the "weapon salve" or "sympathetic powder," which he believed operated through a "magnetic" spiritual force connecting all things.</note>, <term>according to the second section of my Mosaic Philosophy</term> <note>The "Mosaic Philosophy" (Philosophia Moysaica) was Fludd's major work, where he argued that all true knowledge and science was contained within the Book of Genesis.</note>. Farewell.

<meta>This page marks the beginning of Robert Fludd’s formal rebuttal against William Foster. Fludd transitions from his introductory epistle to a structured, point-by-point refutation of Foster’s book, "Hoplocrisma-Spongus: or, A Sponge to wipe away the Weapon-Salve" (1631). Here, Fludd begins to dismantle Foster's argument that magnetic healing is a form of witchcraft.</meta>

Fol. 3.
A 3

<image-desc>A rectangular decorative woodcut headpiece showing two figures amidst scrolling vines and floral patterns, with a central mask or animal face.</image-desc>

# THE SQUEEZING OF FOSTER’S SPONGE.

## PART ONE.

->In which the supposed power of Mr. Foster’s sponge to wipe away, uproot, and delete the <term>weapon salve</term> <note>original: "unguentum armarium"; a controversial medicinal ointment applied not to the wound, but to the weapon that caused it.</note> is itself squeezed out and utterly destroyed, so that the wounded reputation of the Weapon Salve may be restored to its former and customary splendor and glory among men.<-

---

### CHAPTER I.

->In this chapter, a response is given to all objections concerning the question brought forth and proposed by the said "sponge-bearer," or Author of the Sponge.<-

The primary aim of the entire work of Mr. Foster is contained in this question, which he proposes in the following manner:

->**THE QUESTION.**<-
->*Is the healing of wounds performed by the Weapon Salve a form of sorcery and therefore illicit?*<-

Mr. Foster confidently asserts that it is; as for me, I must just as seriously deny it. He offers to prove his point in two ways: first, naturally, through natural philosophy; and second, supernaturally, through theological and ecclesiastical testimonies. Let us see, therefore, how it might be proven to be <term>sorcery</term> <note>original: "veneficium"; in this context, it implies a harmful magical act or witchcraft.</note> using the rules of theology and reasons drawn from the storehouse of Nature.

### His primary arguments in his first article.

<margin>Foster’s objections, page 4.</margin>
All lawful medicines produce their effect either by Divine institution—such as Naaman’s washing in the Jordan to cure leprosy, or the Pool of Bethesda healing those who felt its waters after they were stirred by an Angel—or by natural operation, through those virtues with which God endowed and adorned the creatures from which these medicines are composed. Examples include the poultice of figs used in the healing of King Hezekiah’s abscess, or the oil and wine with which the wounded man was restored with the help of the Samaritan.

->*But this Weapon Salve operates in neither of these ways. Therefore: The healings performed by it are not lawful, but are deceptive, magical, and diabolical.*<-

**Fludd.**
The <term>minor premise</term> <note>In a logical syllogism, the "minor premise" is the second statement; Fludd denies Foster's claim that the salve lacks divine or natural basis.</note> is denied.

**Foster.**
I prove it in two ways: first, it is not by divine institution because it is cited nowhere in the sacred scriptures; second, it does not work naturally because it operates in a manner different from all other agents. For there is a Rule among theologians and philosophers that "no agent acts at a distance." <note>original: "nullum agens agere ad distans"; a fundamental principle in Aristotelian physics which Fludd challenges with his theory of magnetism.</note> Anything that acts does so either by virtual or natural contact; but this weapon salve operates by neither method.

<summary>This page introduces the formal debate between Fludd and Foster, presenting Foster's central argument that the "weapon salve" must be diabolical because it is neither mentioned in the Bible nor follows the Aristotelian rule that objects cannot influence each other from a distance.</summary>

<keywords>Robert Fludd, William Foster, weapon salve, sympathetic magic, action at a distance, natural philosophy, theological debate, Aristotelian physics</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the debate between the physician-philosopher Robert Fludd and the parson William Foster. Fludd is responding to Foster’s logical "syllogisms" (formal arguments) intended to prove the Weapon Salve is demonic because it supposedly violates the laws of physics. Fludd uses both biblical examples and natural phenomena (like magnetism and cannonballs) to argue that God’s power and nature’s mysteries are broader than Foster’s narrow logic allows.</meta>

FOSTER'S SPONGES

manner, Therefore it does not work naturally: It does not work by physical contact, for the bodies <note>The "bodies" refers to the weapon and the wounded person.</note> are disjointed and removed to 20 miles or more: Wherefore (if it be permitted) it is absolutely necessary that it be performed by <term>virtual contact</term> <note>original: "virtuali contactu"; influence exerted through a force or power rather than physical touching.</note>: But not even in this way, because all agents operating in this manner work within a certain distant and determined <term>sphere of activity</term>. A magnet works only from a modest distance.

### *Fludd.*

To your first reason I respond: it does not follow that because it is not cited in the Sacred Scriptures, it is therefore not from divine ordinance and institution. What? Because figs, wine, oil, and even mud mixed with spit are noted in Scripture as external medicines; should the use of caustics, blistering agents, healing by spitting, and similar external medicines—daily applied by Christian physicians—be held as illicit, magical, and diabolical because no mention of them is made in the Scriptures? Or indeed, is nothing created and ordained by God except what the Scripture cites? How then can that saying of the Apostle be verified, that *God works all* <margin>1 Cor. 1:12</margin> *in all*? If he works all things and in all people, he also works all acts and operations, both those hidden and mystical as well as those manifest and apparent to the senses: And therefore all acts are instituted by God: Because, according to Scripture, what God does not wish, he does not do, but when it pleases him and according to his will, he works in the heavens and on the earth: Just as he instituted nothing except what ought to be brought to pass and put into action: So nothing in this whole universe is brought to pass which he himself does not will, institute, and decree: Wherefore the Apostle says in conclusion: *From him, through him,* <margin>Rom. 11</margin> *and in him are all things.* But I shall explain and settle this more widely when I treat this question negatively—namely, where I am to prove that the weapon salve is lawful and not at all "evil-magic."

To your second argument I reply, that it is of no greater weight or efficacy than the first. The universal axiom of the common Philosophers, upon which you found your proof to exclude this ointment from the realm of nature, is this: *No agent acts at a distance.* From this you fashion such an argument:

### *Foster.*

Whatever works naturally, works by physical or virtual contact. But this works by neither of these, Therefore it does not work naturally.

### *Fludd.*

First, as concerns that stated Axiom in Philosophy, I could indeed prove it false by my own experience: For fire heats at a distance: Lightning from a cloud strikes at a distance: The laurel tree works against the power of thunder at a distance: The Sun and fire act by illuminating at a distance: The force of cannonballs reaches out without contact at a distance: The magnet acts and works upon iron at a distance. Epidemics, dysentery, and measles kill at a distance, etc. But so that you may satisfy this, you add to the Axiom, saying: *It acts either by physical or by virtual contact*, instead of saying, *it acts at a distance*: I will respond first that the <term>major premise</term> is weakened: For I would have you know that lightning can move the air violently, and the air, set in motion by the contact of the agent (namely the lightning), can stun and bring about death: So that in this case there is neither virtual nor physical contact of the agent, but an accidental event between the virtual agent and the patient: A similar thing appears in cannonballs, which, flying near someone without any virtual or physical contact, strike a man down: For the agent, namely the ball, moves the medium (that is, the air) violently, and the air thus moved casts the man down: But, though I might pass over the probability of the Major premise, nevertheless the <term>minor premise</term> is entirely crippled: For I assert, and it is clear to the capacity of any man’s wit, that this medicine heals by virtual contact—namely, through a <term>magnetic property</term>, which works between the starting point and the destination, between the Beginning and the end, magnetically, secretly, and mystically.

### *Foster.*

The minor premise or assumption is proven thus:

All agents operating by virtual contact work within a certain distance and a determined sphere of activity: A magnet acts upon iron by virtual contact, but only at a modest distance: Fire is the most furious agent of all, but a fire the size of ten miles cannot burn or heat a man two miles distant and remote: The planets excel all sublunary agents in virtual operation: The light of the sun passes through the whole world, yet meanwhile, a small cloud obscures its light and weakens its heat. The Earth intercepts and excludes light from the <term>Antipodes</term> <note>People living on the opposite side of the earth.</note>: The body of the Moon eclipses the sun: If, therefore, terrestrial agents are entirely hindered by distance or interposition, and celestial ones are hindered in part, shall this weapon salve work from the weapon to the wound at any and every distance? Will not the interposition of air, forests, fire, water, mountains, walls, buildings, castles, cities, heat, or cold at all delay the derivation of its power and efficacy?

<meta>catchword: Fludd.</meta>

<summary>Fludd refutes Foster's claim that the Weapon Salve is non-natural, arguing that many natural forces like magnetism, heat, and disease act at a distance. He challenges Foster's rigid logic by citing both biblical precedents for non-scriptural medicines and physical phenomena that bypass direct contact.</summary>
<keywords>Robert Fludd, William Foster, weapon salve, action at a distance, virtual contact, magnetism, biblical medicine, sphere of activity, physics vs magic</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the heated debate between the physician Robert Fludd and the parson William Foster regarding the "Weapon Salve"—a medicinal ointment applied to the weapon that caused a wound rather than the wound itself. Foster argues this is impossible by natural laws, while Fludd appeals to the subtle "vital spirits" that connect all living things.</meta>

### EXPLANATION OF PART I. Folio 4.
A 4
<meta>catchword: (I say)</meta>

->**Fludd.**<-

How much trouble our censor causes us in a matter so ill-suited to our scope and so foreign to our purpose! Indeed, I thought he would proceed by <term>syllogism</term> <note>A formal logical argument consisting of a major premise, a minor premise, and a conclusion.</note> until the very end of his arguments, just as he began. But I see that so many obstacles have hindered his progress in proving his proposition that, like a <note>original: "equae lassatae"</note> tired mare, he gives up his method of demonstration in the middle of the road. Therefore, it is necessary to treat this long-winded response of his in the text as dregs, so that I may respond to each of his points individually, both hastily and in a mixed fashion.

->**Foster.**<-

All efficient agents operate through <term>virtual contact</term> <note>original: "contactu virtuali"; a philosophical term for an object's power acting upon another without physical touching, like a magnet.</note> within a certain distance and within a determined <term>sphere of activity</term>. A magnet acts upon iron by virtual contact, but it only works within a modest distance.

->**Fludd.**<-

Who asserts that any virtual contact can operate unto infinity, since the world itself is finite? But by your leave, my Lord: 그 that very special virtue completes its operation either further away or closer, according as it is exalted in its actual power and essence. For example: a certain kind of <term>gunpowder</term> <note>original: "pulveris tormentarii"</note> propels things further and to a greater distance than another; this lightning bolt penetrates more deeply from above than that one—so much so that it has been observed not only to penetrate deeply into hard crags or rocks, but even to strike a solid sword resting in its scabbard and melt it, while other fires or bolts from above come up short in their virtual contact. Similarly, we find this magnet to be of greater efficacy and agility in operating than that one; and accordingly, it attracts iron to itself at a greater interval as well as with greater force and efficacy. Does not Scripture teach that God distributed his gifts to some more abundantly and generously, and to others more sparingly? He even produced certain lower creatures in one and the same genus that are more virtuous in acting than others. For it becomes us to observe constantly (since you speak of a "sphere of activity") that the more virtuous a central agent is in any thing, the larger its semi-diameters will be, and consequently its circumference. 

For example: the more effective a fire is, the further and more distantly it will cast its heat in a circle. Thus, the sphere of activity of that very agent will by no means be fixed in its species, but longer or shorter according to the power of the agent itself, and consequently it does not observe any "determined" sphere of activity. Finally, Mr. Foster has very little knowledge of that admirable faculty of the <term>vital spirits</term> <note>original: "spirituum vitalium"; in Fludd’s philosophy, the subtle, invisible substance that carries life through the body and can extend beyond it.</note> of man, whether dilated or emitted; nor can it in any way be compared with the weak power of common creatures or <term>elemental fire</term> <note>Standard fire found on earth, like a hearth fire, as opposed to celestial or spiritual fire.</note>. It is an influence subtle in its purity and penetration, so penetrable indeed—or rather, acting more freely—than the influence of any star in the sky. It is not hindered by clouds, nor intercepted by walls or mountains; it is an essential force proceeding radically from God, who animates it, moving it always into the medium of the air. Do you not safely acknowledge this when you say with the Apostle: *In God we live, move, and have our being*? <margin>Acts 17:28</margin> But let us proceed.

->**Foster.**<-

Fire is the most furious agent of all; but a fire within the limits of ten miles cannot burn or warm a man standing two miles away.

->**Fludd.**<-

I can hardly give you credit here, my Foster, for I am certain you would find a larger sphere of activity in such a proportion of fire than your body could endure without being scorched. For, if the force of unity is stronger, would you have a fire ten miles wide that does not warm—nay, rather, does not sear and burn—at two miles distance? Again, pardon me, Sir, for I say your artificial fire is not the most rapid agent of all: for lightning from heaven is much more powerful in its operation than our artificial fire. For although it may have an area less than a hundredth part of ten miles, yet it so inflames the air for several miles that it provokes sweat in creatures from the heat. 

Furthermore, <term>Olympic fire</term> <note>The pure, celestial fire of the stars and heavens.</note> or stellar fire excels this by many <term>parasangs</term> <note>An ancient Persian unit of distance, used here to mean "by a long shot."</note>, so that it enters and penetrates many degrees further than elemental fire can. For although elemental fire abounds in activity, yet it is endowed with a spirit so thick in which it is carried; and although it is subtle and contains the source of heavenly fire within itself, yet because it cannot be moved except in its own medium or "thick vehicle"—namely artificial fire—it is hindered. Without the gross smoke of combustible, natural, and elemental matter, and without the clouds and air in which it is carried, it is prevented from completing such a large sphere or diameter in its activity. Because its vehicle is gross, it cannot penetrate the air without obstacle. But heavenly fire, which is the source of the fire of life, by reason of its subtle spirit (which is its own ethereal vehicle), penetrates all things. Since it is nothing but a subtle influence, which (as the wiser Philosophers assert) penetrates crags and stones without obstacle—nay, even the very center of the earth, as experience itself testifies. For otherwise (say the Philosophers) this influence could not gradually produce metallic forms and precious stones in the bowels of the earth, more or less noble according to the dignity of that stellar spirit which sends forth that influence and the purity of that <term>Mercurial vapor</term> <note>A term from alchemy referring to the subtle, fluid principle from which metals were thought to grow.</note> which it animates. But yet the life of man, that purified spirit...

<summary>Fludd refutes Foster's claim that natural forces have fixed limits, arguing that "vital spirits" and "celestial fire" can penetrate physical barriers like walls and mountains to act at a distance. He uses examples of gunpowder, magnets, and lightning to show that an agent's "sphere of activity" depends on its inherent power rather than a set physical rule.</summary>
<keywords>virtual contact, sphere of activity, vital spirits, magnetism, celestial fire, Olympic fire, alchemy, weapon salve, Robert Fludd, William Foster</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s systematic rebuttal of William Foster’s attack on the "weapon-salve." Fludd is currently addressing Foster’s logical syllogisms, specifically the argument that healing at a distance must be demonic because it defies the known laws of physics.</meta>

...in this manner: Therefore, it does not work naturally. It does not work by physical contact, for the bodies are separate and distant by twenty miles or more. Therefore (if it were allowed to be lawful), it would necessarily have to be performed by **virtual contact** <note>In the 17th century, "virtual contact" referred to an influence or "virtue" (power) acting through space without physical touching, like a magnet's pull.</note>. But it cannot work even in this way, because all agents working in this manner operate only within a certain distance and a determined sphere of activity. A magnet, for instance, works only from a modest distance.

->*Fludd.*<-

<margin>1 Corinthians 1:12.</margin>
<margin>Romans 2.</margin>
To your first reason, I respond that it does not follow that just because something is not cited in the Holy Scriptures, it is therefore not from divine ordinance and institution. What then? Because figs, wine, oil, and even mud tempered and mixed with spit are noted in Scripture as external medicines, should the use of <term>caustics</term> <note>Burning agents used to treat wounds.</note>, <term>vesicatories</term> <note>Medicines that create blisters to draw out "bad humors."</note>, healing by spitting, and similar external medicines applied daily by Christian physicians be held as illicit, magical, and diabolical simply because no mention of them is made in the Scriptures? Or is nothing truly destined and ordained by God unless the Scripture cites it?

How then can that word of the Apostle be verified: *God works all things in all?* <note>original: "Deum operari omnia in omnibus"; see 1 Corinthians 12:6.</note> If he works all things and in all people, he also works all acts and operations, both those hidden and mystical as well as those manifest and apparent to the senses. And therefore, all acts are instituted by God. For, according to Scripture, what God does not want, he does not do; rather, he works in the heavens and on earth whenever it pleases him and according to his will. Thus, just as he instituted nothing except that which ought to be brought into effect and action, so nothing in this whole universe is brought to pass that he himself does not will, institute, and decree. Whence the Apostle says in conclusion: *From him, through him, and in him are all things.* <note>original: "Ab ipso, per ipsum, et in ipso sunt omnia"; see Romans 11:36.</note> But I shall explain and clear this up more broadly when I treat this question in the negative—namely, where I shall prove that the **weapon-salve** is lawful and not in the least <term>cacomagical</term> <note>original: "cacomagicum"; derived from Greek, meaning "evil magic" or "black magic."</note>.

To your second argument, I reply that it is of no greater weight or efficacy than the first. The **universal axiom** <note>original: "Axioma Catholicum"; here "Catholic" is used in its original sense of "universal" or "general."</note> of common philosophers, upon which you base your proof to exclude this ointment from the realm of nature, is this: *No agent acts at a distance.* From this, you fashion such an argument:

->*Foster.*<-

Whatever works naturally works by physical or virtual contact. But this salve works by neither of these. Therefore, it does not work naturally.

->*Fludd.*<-

Firstly, regarding that stated axiom in Philosophy, I could indeed prove it false by my own experience. For fire heats at a distance; lightning from a cloud strikes at a distance; the laurel tree works against the power of thunder at a distance; the sun and fire act by illuminating at a distance. The force of cannonballs reaches out without contact at a distance. A magnet acts and works upon iron at a distance. Epidemics, dysentery, and measles kill at a distance, and so forth.

But to satisfy this, you add to the axiom, saying: *It acts either by physical or virtual contact*, in place of "it acts at a distance." I will respond first that your <term>Major Premise</term> <note>In logic, the first part of a formal argument (syllogism).</note> is weakened. For I would have you know that lightning can move the air violently, and the air, once moved by the contact of the agent (namely, the lightning), can stun and bring death. Thus, in this case, there is neither virtual nor physical contact by the agent, but an accidental event between the virtual agent and the patient. Something similar appears in cannonballs, which, flying near someone without any virtual or physical contact, strike a man down. For the agent (the ball) moves the medium (the air) violently, and the air thus moved casts the man down. But, even if I let the probability of your Major Premise pass, the <term>Minor Premise</term> <note>The second part of a formal argument.</note> is nonetheless completely crippled. For I assert—and it is clear to the capacity of any man’s wit—that this medicine heals by **virtual contact**, namely through a **Magnetic property**, which works between the point of origin and the point of destination, between the beginning and the end, magnetically, occultly, and mystically.

->*Foster.*<-

The Minor Premise or assumption is proven thus:
All agents working by virtual contact operate within a certain distance and a determined sphere of activity. A magnet acts on iron by virtual contact, but only at a modest distance. Fire is the most furious agent of all, yet a fire ten miles in size cannot burn or heat a man two miles distant and remote. The planets excel all sublunary agents in their virtual operation; the light of the sun passes through the whole world, yet meanwhile, a small cloud obscures its light and weakens its heat. The Earth intercepts and shuts out the light from the Antipodes. The body of the Moon eclipses the sun. Shall, therefore, terrestrial agents be completely hindered by distance or interposition, and celestial ones be partially hindered, while this weapon-salve shall operate from the weapons to the wound at any and every distance? Will not the interposition of air, woods, fire, water, mountains, walls, buildings, castles, cities, heat, or cold delay the derivation of its virtue and efficacy at all?

<summary>Robert Fludd refutes William Foster's claim that healing at a distance is impossible in nature. Fludd argues that many natural phenomena, like magnets and lightning, act at a distance and suggests the weapon-salve works through a "virtual" magnetic contact ordained by God.</summary>

<keywords>weapon-salve, action at a distance, virtual contact, magnetism, Robert Fludd, William Foster, natural philosophy, theology vs science, optics, mechanics of air</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the heated debate between Robert Fludd and William Foster. Fludd is responding to Foster’s claim that all natural forces must operate within a limited "sphere of activity." Fludd argues that spiritual and celestial forces, such as the human "vital spirit" or "celestial fire," are far more powerful and penetrating than ordinary "elemental" fire, allowing them to act across great distances.</meta>

EXPLANATION OF SECTION I. Leaf 4.
A 4
<meta>catchword: (I say)</meta>

->**Fludd.**<-

How much trouble our censor <note>Fludd is referring to William Foster, who had criticized Fludd's support of the Weapon Salve.</note> causes us in a matter so ill-suited to our goal and foreign to our purpose! Indeed, I thought he would proceed syllogistically <note>Using formal logic and structured arguments.</note> right to the end of his arguments, just as he began. But I see him encounter so many obstacles in his path toward proving his proposition that, like a tired mare <note>original: "equæ lassatæ"</note>, he gives up his method of demonstration in the middle of the road. Therefore, I must pick through this rambling response of his—this dreg of a text—to respond to each of his points quickly and in whatever order they appear.

->**Foster.**<-

All efficient agents <note>In Aristotelian physics, an "efficient agent" is the thing that causes a change to happen.</note> work through <term>virtual contact</term> <note>Influence exerted through a force or power rather than physical touching.</note> within a certain distance and within a determined <term>sphere of activity</term>. A magnet acts on iron through virtual contact, but it works only within a modest distance.

->**Fludd.**<-

Who asserts that any virtual contact can operate unto infinity, since the world itself is finite? But by your leave, my Lord: that very specific power completes its operation either further away or closer at hand, depending on how it is exalted in its actual power and essence. For example: one kind of gunpowder <note>original: "pulveris tormentarij"</note> propels further and more distantly than another; one bolt of lightning penetrates deeper from above than another; so much so that it has even been observed not only to penetrate deeply into hard crags or rocks, but even to strike a solid sword while it was inside its scabbard and melt it, while other fires or lightning bolts from above had come more quickly from their virtual contact. Similarly, we find that this magnet is of greater efficacy and agility in its work than that one; and accordingly, it attracts iron to itself at a longer interval and with greater force and efficacy. Does not the Scripture teach that God distributed his gifts more abundantly and generously to some, and more sparingly to others? Just as He also produced certain lower creatures within one and the same genus that are more powerful in action than others. For it becomes us to observe constantly (since you speak of a sphere of activity) that the more powerful the central agent is in any thing, the larger will be its radii and, consequently, its circumference. For example: the more effective the fire, the further and more distantly it will cast its heat in a circle. Thus, the sphere of activity of that very agent in its specific form will be in no way fixed, but will be longer or shorter according to the power of the agent itself; consequently, it does not observe any "determined" sphere of activity. Finally, Mr. Foster has very little knowledge of the wonderful faculty of the human vital spirits, when they are dilated or sent forth; nor can they be compared in any way to the weak power of common creatures or elemental fire. It is an influence subtle in its purity and penetration, so penetrable indeed—or rather, a freer influence—than that of any star in heaven; it is not hindered by clouds, nor intercepted by walls or mountains. It is an essential force proceeding fundamentally from God, who animates it, moving it always into the medium of the air. Do you not acknowledge this yourself when you say with the Apostle: *In God we live, move, and have our being*? <note>Acts 17:28</note> But let us move on.

->**Foster.**<-

Fire is the most furious agent of all: But fire within the limits of ten miles cannot burn, or even warm, a man standing two miles away.

->**Fludd.**<-

I can hardly give you credit, my Foster, for I am certain you will find a larger sphere of activity in such a proportion of fire than your body could sustain without being scorched. For, if a united force is stronger, will you have a fire ten miles wide that does not warm—nay, rather, does not scorch and burn—at two miles distance? Again, forgive me, sir, for I say your artificial fire is not the most rapid agent of all: for lightning from heaven is much more powerful in its operation than our artificial fire. For although it may cover a smaller area than a hundredth part of ten miles, yet it so inflames the air for several miles that it draws sweat from creatures by its heat. Furthermore, the <term>Aelympic</term> <note>A variant of "Olympic," referring here to heavenly or celestial fire.</note> or stellar fire excels this by so many leagues that it passes and penetrates through many degrees further than elemental fire can. For although elemental fire abounds in activity, yet it is endowed with such a thick spirit in which it is carried; so that, although it is subtle and contains the source of celestial fire within itself, yet because it cannot move except in its own medium or "thick vehicle"—namely artificial fire, lower down, without the gross smoke of combustible matter, both natural and elemental, without the clouds and air in which it is carried—for this reason it is hindered from completing such a large sphere or diameter in its activity. Because the vehicle being thick, it cannot penetrate into the air without obstacle. But celestial fire, which is the source of the fire of life, by reason of its subtle spirit (which is its own ethereal vehicle), penetrates all things, since it is nothing but a subtle influence which, as the wiser philosophers assert, penetrates through crags and stones without obstacle—nay, even to the very center of the earth, as experience itself testifies. For otherwise (say the philosophers) this influence could not gradually produce metallic forms and precious stones in the bowels of the earth, which are more or less noble according to the dignity of the stellar spirit that sends in that influence and the purity of that <term>Mercurial vapor</term> <note>In alchemy, mercury was considered a fundamental component of all metals; "mercurial vapor" refers to the spiritualized matter from which metals grow.</note> which it animates. But yet the life of man, that purified spirit...

<summary>Robert Fludd refutes William Foster's claim that natural forces have fixed limits, arguing that spiritual and celestial influences (like the human vital spirit or lightning) are far more penetrating and powerful than ordinary "elemental" fire. Fludd uses examples from magnetism, gunpowder, and the formation of metals in the earth to show that the range of an influence depends on the power of its source.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s spirited defense of the "Weapon Salve" against the criticisms of the parson William Foster. Fludd is responding to Foster’s argument that physical distance and obstacles (like walls or mountains) should logically block the medicinal effect of the salve. Fludd counters by appealing to the Hermetic and Neoplatonic idea of "spiritual influence," which he argues operates on a higher plane than mere physical objects.</meta>

# FOSTER’S SPONGES.

(I say) by which man lives, is more subtle, purer, and more exalted than that <note>referring to the vital spirit of other creatures or elements mentioned previously</note>; and consequently of greater activity, as will be demonstrated below.

## Foster.
The stars excel all sublunary <term>agents</term> <note>An "agent" in 17th-century philosophy is any force or substance that produces an effect.</note>; the light of the sun passes through the entire world, yet nonetheless even a small cloud obscures its light and weakens its heat. The Earth holds back and shuts out the light from the <term>Antipodes</term> <note>The people or places on the exact opposite side of the globe.</note>, the body of the moon eclipses the Sun, etc.

## Fludd.
If the celestial stars excel sublunary agents, Mr. Foster falls into a truly just and worthy rebuke, for he said just a little while ago that terrestrial fire was the most violent and rapid agent of all. But he attacks me in this way: For if celestial agents are more powerful than sublunary ones, it suggests that they have a larger <term>sphere of activity</term> <note>The physical area within which a force, such as magnetism or heat, can be felt.</note> and can emit direct rays much further than a magnet, fire, or similar terrestrial things. In these sublunary things, although the agent is celestial, it is nonetheless so clouded and hindered by a thick spirit and a compact body that it cannot operate. This is apparent in a grain of wheat: its internal and central fire, unless it is restored to liberty, will not move or rise from the surface of the earth toward heaven to multiply itself. 

But to the point. What story is our censor telling about the Sun, the light of the Sun, the Eclipse, the Earth coming between the Sun on our horizon and the antipodes, or the blocking of light by a cloud? By Hercules! This proves his own thinness and ignorance in matters of Astronomy and Philosophy more than it actually touches upon anything relevant to our argument. For I trust it is not unknown to him that, besides light, there are two other things that must be exactly considered by Philosophers: namely, **motion** and **influence**. 

If you wish to assert that <term>influence</term> <note>In Fludd’s philosophy, "influence" is an invisible, spiritual flow from the stars that affects life on Earth, distinct from visible light.</note> can be intercepted by little clouds, or by the placement of stars, air, water, or earth, you are immediately in error, and you have not even "tasted philosophy with the tip of your lips" <note>original: "nec Philosophiam primis labris degustasti"; a Latin idiom meaning to have only a superficial knowledge.</note>. The stars operate by motion, light, and influence—not by light alone. Visible light can be obscured from us, but influence flows without obstacle. And in this opinion are the wiser Philosophers of a "cleaner nose" <note>original: "emunctioris naris"; an idiom meaning someone with sharp discernment or good taste.</note>.

## Foster.
Will terrestrial agents then be hindered in every way by distance or obstacles, while celestial ones are only partially hindered? And will this "Weapon Salve" operate from the weapons upon the wound at any distance? Will the placement of air, woods, fire, water, walls, buildings, castles, cities, or mountains, I say, in no way hinder the transmission of that virtue?

## Fludd.
First, I assert that the original act in this cure proceeds from the wounded man to the ointment, and not (as he says) from the ointment to the wound. Next, to this "good man" so occupied and busy, who makes so much trouble out of nothing, I replied not long ago: because this Spirit proceeds from a celestial influence animated by God, it therefore does not have such obstacles or impediments. This subtle thing is in no way hindered by any subtle creatures in its descent to nourish that <term>species</term> for which it was ordained and destined from creation. 

But we must know only this: that before it descended, it was **Universal** <note>original: "Catholicum"; meaning all-encompassing or general.</note>; but after it penetrated our bodies, it took on a **special and particular** nature, and it has a special sympathy with a nature similar to itself. For this reason, the spirit of the wounded man penetrates through the vehicle of the air (in which the blood is applied to the Ointment) and is carried to the Ointment by a natural affection—all the more so because blood was air, and air is "dilated blood" in its external form, which I can demonstrate to the eye. Similarly, the principal ingredient of the ointment was blood. 

Therefore, just as we see the Sun emit its spirit through its rays into a grain of wheat in the earth, and see its living influence (or the continuous essential rays of its emission) maintain a connection with its like—indeed, with that very thing which lies hidden in the dead and corrupt grain, and so gradually brings back to life that which was as if dead and buried in corruption, making it grow and flourish with multiplication—in the very same way, the **Sun of Life** <note>The "Sun of Life" refers to the vital, divine spark within a human being, mirroring the Sun in the heavens.</note> living in man and still moving in man (after the manner of the macrocosmic Sun) has continuous living rays of influence connected with the spiritual spark in the dead blood. This spark is the same as the emitting influence, but it is buried in the dead bodily "grain"—namely, the dead blood carried to the Ointment. 

We compare this ointment to very rich, fertile, and fruitful earth, as it is better suited for the nutrition of that hidden spirit which lies concealed in the blood. Since those things—the Ointment and the blood—when transferred or committed to it, are not foreign to each other but <term>homogeneous</term>, or rather equally familiar, being one specific body for a spirit of the same degree in nature. For insofar as the body of the Ointment is composed (according to my recipe) of blood, fatty flesh, and the "vines" or outgrowths of bones <note>Fludd is likely referring to "mummy" or moss grown on human remains, common in these recipes.</note> of the same <term>microcosmic species</term> (though not the same individual), all of these are animated by that spirit residing in human blood. Therefore, the influence of life proceeding from the microcosmic or human Sun, and assisting, reviving, and multiplying...

<meta>catchword: ...multiplying</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd refutes William Foster's claim that physical obstacles should block the Weapon Salve's effects. Fludd argues that the salve works via a "spiritual influence" similar to the Sun's ability to revive a seed, which transcends physical barriers like walls or distance.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense of the "Weapon Salve" (the medicinal ointment applied to a weapon to heal the wound it caused). Fludd is responding to William Foster's previous point about the "grain of life" found in blood. Fludd argues that this vital spirit is "excited" by the ointment just as the sun excites life in the earth during spring.</meta>

# THE EXPRESSION OF THE FIRST MEMBER

### B

...gradually multiplying the hidden grain of life, which is in the blood now removed and placed in the ointment, and also rousing the potential or "stunned" spirit within the Ointment. This is done in no different a way than we see the celestial Sun in the springtime rouse the spirits of the earth, which the frozen winter had stunned. Between these two extremes <note>The "extremes" likely refer to the physical distance between the wounded person and the weapon-salve on the blade.</note>, it does not cease to work until the person recovers.

This is the true mystery of the question, and I will maintain it: that the use of the earth for putrefying <note>In 17th-century alchemy and medicine, "putrefaction" was a necessary stage of decay that allowed for new life or spiritual "multiplication" to occur.</note>, rousing, and multiplying the grain is only "magical," diabolic, and illicit if it can be truly demonstrated that the use of this Ointment is sorcery <note>original: "veneficium"</note> and entirely intolerable. For the mystery of their multiplication, as well as the revivification and the conjunction of the life-giving spirit, is one and the same as the spirit of another person being brought back to life. This was the Type by which Saint Paul teaches us about the resurrection—namely, through the mortification and putrefaction of grain in the earth—and as far as the operation of the Sun is concerned in the multiplication of grain, every plowman or farmer will instruct you on that. Therefore, to conclude this: we see the sun is sometimes further away and sometimes nearer, and yet it does not cease to work, more or less, by causing growth and multiplication in animals, vegetables, and metals. I must now hear Foster’s own conclusion.

->Foster<-

O <term>agent</term> of agents! Truly the Angels of the heavens can scarcely work at such a distance: God Alone, whose essence is infinite, who is all in all, can work in this way, because nothing is distant from Him at all: for in Him we live, move, and have our being;

<margin>Acts 17</margin>

->Fludd<-

Cease your wondering: it contributes nothing to our text. You have only revealed your own absurdity. I wish you would give up your attempt to abolish and "wipe out" our weapon salve; instead, use our "sponge" <note>Fludd’s book was titled "The Squeezing of Foster’s Sponge," a satirical response to Foster’s own book "The Hop-locrisma-spongus" (A Sponge to Wipe Away the Weapon-Salve).</note> to root out and delete the stains of your own error, which you have fully expressed in this assertion of yours. For by this reasoning, you have overturned the very state of your controversy. You say indeed, after your excessive wondering, that the Angels of heaven cannot work at such a distance. Therefore, I conclude in this way: then much less can the infernal Angels <note>Demons</note>, for they are darker and thus have less reach. 

Now, you said before that a cloud intercepts the light of the Sun; therefore, by Hercules, the devil—who is an Angel of darkness—must necessarily be much more hindered in his virtual operations, and consequently in the extension of his power, than the Angels of light. But it is the power of the devil, you say, that causes this ointment to produce such effects at so great a distance, for otherwise it would not be sorcery or diabolical. Thus, you then infer for me: Only God, whose essence is infinite, and who is all in all, can act in this way, etc. Is that so? And for that reason, Mr. Foster attributes this act to the devil—the worst and most wicked of the Angels—and thus he does not blush to commit more than common Idolatry by arrogating and imputing to a creature, even to the devil himself, that which by his own confession belongs to God. Will you confess that He is "all in all," and yet you establish the devil as being "more all" in the happiness of healing than God Himself? Or will you acknowledge with the Apostle that God works all in all, and will you then attribute and ascribe His effect (the fruit of which is goodness) to the devil, whom He predestined and ordained for punishing, destroying, and corrupting, but truly not for healing? Do you not conclude absolutely in favor of the weapon salve by saying: "In him we live, move, etc."? 

O, by the faith of Gods and men! What an error this is in such a notable <term>Philosophaster</term> <note>A "philosophaster" is a pretender or a fake philosopher; Fludd is insulting Foster's intellectual credentials.</note>, or rather, in a "Theosophist"! Let the world perceive from these things that some things seem to be and are not. But let me proceed further to the conclusion.

->Foster<-

Let the reader, powerful in judgment and piety, judge therefore whether the doctors of the weapon salve do not fashion a God out of their ointment, and whether they do not commit Idolatry by attributing to a tiny bit of liniment of their own making that which is proper only to God, the maker of all things.

->Fludd<-

You are mistaken, sir; they do not make a God out of their ointment, but they give immortal thanks to Him for that blessed gift of miraculous healing which He has bestowed upon this ointment. Nor did the Jews attribute the property of healing to the marsh or the Pool of Bethesda, but to the healing of God or to the health-bringing Angel who imparted that gift to it. Therefore, I wish you, sir, and moreover the whole world, to know well that every suspicion of Idolatry in this case is removed by the doctors: for as much as the due acknowledgment and veneration is ascribed by them to God alone for His kindness manifested in the healing through this ointment. Indeed, rather the title of "idolater" ought not undeservedly be imputed to you yourself, Sir, who dare so profanely and impiously to impute these kind, curative blessings of God to the devil, the most wicked of creatures.

Again, we deny that it is an artificial composition fashioned by human hand that heals, but rather the natural ingredients of the composition, which God originally adorned and gifted with hidden and mystical virtue for the sake of healing. To finally conclude this point: if the reader is strong in judgment and weighs well the words of Foster’s text, he will find him therein to be an Apostate or a heretic according to his own doctrine.

<summary>Fludd argues that the weapon salve works through natural, God-given virtues rather than demonic magic, accusing his opponent Foster of idolatry for attributing God's healing powers to the Devil.</summary>
<keywords>weapon salve, Robert Fludd, William Foster, idolatry, healing, vital spirit, Acts 17, Pool of Bethesda, nature, divine virtue</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s spirited defense against William Foster. Fludd is mocking Foster’s inconsistent arguments regarding the "Weapon Salve"—a controversial medical treatment where an ointment was applied to the weapon that caused a wound, rather than the wound itself, to facilitate healing through a perceived "magnetic" or "spiritual" connection.</meta>

# FOSTER’S SPONGES.

For his assertion, which he seems to support in a giant-like <note>original: "more gyganteo"—meaning with great force or arrogance, like the mythological giants challenging the gods.</note> manner, is that the **Weapon Ointment** <note>original: "Unguentum armarium"</note> is Diabolical, or an effect produced by the subtle craftsmanship of Satan. Yet, in this text, he speaks hesitantly and waveringly, saying that physicians attribute to a modest ointment or liniment what belongs to God alone. If this were true (as indeed it is), then **Mr. Foster** is caught in an abominable error, for he is the one who claims this cure is solely the act and work of the Devil.

And in this way (kind reader) you see the effectiveness of this man’s arguments, both Philosophical and Theological. From his ignorance of the cause, he seems to cloak the goodness of God with a deceptive mask of Satan. You see his lack of credibility. But, just as repenting sinners turn themselves from the Devil to God at the end, so (by the grace of God) the adversary of this Weapon Ointment—led by a good spirit rather than his own will—concludes truly, saying that it is not good Angels, and therefore much less the Devil, who can bring about such an effect, but God alone. I certainly rejoice in his conversion, even if it is against his will. Behold, how he now agrees with his greatest enemy, that "damned" **Paracelsus** (as he calls him), who affirms that it is a gift of God! As for the authors he cites against it, I certainly do not look up to them; there are just as many on the other side of better authority and judgment. Indeed, they are not your typical "schoolmen" <note>Scholastic philosophers who focused on logic and abstract theory rather than physical experimentation.</note> who labor only in imaginary, speculative Philosophy; nor are they **Jean Roberti the Jesuit**, and similar fantastic theorists, but learned physicians and distinguished Philosophers, well-versed in the mysteries of nature both theoretically and practically. For that reason, they are far more suitable subjects and instruments for discussing this matter of physical nature. Among these, I appeal in the first place to **Bishop Anselm**, who for his integrity, distinguished learning, and piety of life was canonized as a saint; and then among the profound Philosophers and physicians who were versed in the mysteries of God and nature: **Theophrastus Paracelsus**, who quite appropriately calls it a gift of God; **Cardano**, **Giambattista della Porta**, **Oswald Croll**, **Johann Ernst Burgrav**, **Rudolph Goclenius**, **Jan Baptist van Helmont**, and many other famous and experienced Philosophers and physicians. Through the practical art of Alchemy (than which there is no science in the world that more clearly reveals and discovers the hidden mysteries of nature to the eye) as well as through other frequent observations founded upon reason—not merely upon imaginary speculation—these men have immersed themselves as true Philosophers into this mystery of healing. These were men, I say, who were just as shrewd at avoiding and cautious in foreseeing the Devil’s deception—indeed, even at distinguishing his act from that act of God in nature—as Mr. Foster or any of his school-masterly teachers. And although some superstitious physicians of this realm (I mean those who are prompter to judge this business perversely than to weigh it with due consideration) seem to oppose it, they cannot be ignorant of the fact that more things lie hidden than are visible.

And therefore, if they are ignorant in this mystery, it would be a sign of their highest wisdom and prudence to remain silent, rather than to gnaw at it with their criticism, holding this for certain and persuaded: that there are more hidden and secret things in nature than fall within the sphere of their capacity. A word to the wise.

Again, I think it is a matter suited for those who have scarcely greeted the principles of Philosophy from the front doorstep, rather than for a confirmed Philosopher, to say regarding the secrets of nature: "He himself said it: therefore it is so" <note>original: "Ipse dixit." A common phrase meaning one accepts something as true simply because an authority figure said it.</note>. For it is human to err. Indeed, this wisdom is most to be approved in a Philosopher: first, to wisely immerse oneself in the divine mysteries in nature; and then afterward, where he is confident, to conclude demonstratively—not from the reports of others, but from his own personal knowledge.

Therefore, since **Mr. Foster** has left nothing untried to annihilate, hiss at, and explode this most noble medicine—to whom I have so far responded in the manner of opposition only superficially and by way of answering his objections—I hope and trust you will grant me leave to also leave nothing untried in expressing and squeezing out from his "dreadful sponge" the reputation of the Weapon Ointment, which he has devoured and swallowed like a <term>cormorant</term> <note>original: "phalacrocoracis." A water bird known for its gluttony; Fludd is using it as a metaphor for how Foster has consumed and then "sponged up" the reputation of the medicine.</note>.

### QUESTION.

->Whether the healing of wounds by the Weapon Ointment is sorcery and illicit or not; first, Theologically.<-

I deny it and prove it <insert>{</insert> <insert>Next, Theo-Philosophically, that is, through pure</insert>
in two ways. <insert>2</insert> <insert>Natural Philosophy.</insert>

<sig>I</sig>

<summary>Robert Fludd defends the "Weapon Salve" against William Foster's accusations of sorcery, citing a long list of famous Renaissance scientists and philosophers as authorities. He argues that the cure is a natural, God-given process revealed through alchemy and observation rather than a diabolical trick.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical and theological defense of the "Weapon Ointment" (a salve applied to a weapon to heal the wound it caused) against the attacks of the minister William Foster. On the previous page, Fludd pointed out that Foster accidentally admitted that only God can act at a distance, effectively undermining his own argument that the ointment’s action is "diabolical."</meta>

Fol. 6.

EXPRESSION OF THE FIRST PART.

### CHAPTER II.

-> Here the power and successful operation of the weapon ointment is proven to be a gift of God; and not indeed any act of the Devil. <-

Mr. Foster claims that **Paracelsus** <note>The Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who popularized the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine.</note> asserts the power of this medicine is a gift of God. For this reason, Foster is very angry with him, calling him a sorcerer, a conjurer, and an evil magician <note>original: "Cacomagum" — from the Greek words for 'evil' and 'magician.'</note>. It may be well thought of him who prepares to preach goodness to an insane man, or an ungrateful one, or one irrationally jealous of someone else’s success. But after Mr. Foster has left nothing untried to prove the use of this ointment is magical, illusory, and diabolical; I indeed hope and trust to demonstrate the opposite on the same foundations, by ascribing the rightful and true ownership to God, the true possessor, and depriving the Devil of those things which he has falsely usurped through his instruments.

Just as before we prepare to build some magnificent and splendid palace, there is a need for laying a very firm and fixed foundation to support such a great structure above; so, before we begin to establish the strongest fortress of defense—which shall stand as a firm negative opposition against my adversary’s affirmation—I think it convenient to collect certain very valid foundations or spiritual arguments. These shall serve as cornerstones to stabilize and support the entire truth of the question at hand, and to express its true resolution: that the truth is entirely contrary and opposite to what he puts forward. Therefore, I will imitate him by entering into this inquiry through this logical argument based on his own confession, which he himself blurted out on page 7. "The angels of heaven," he says, "cannot work at such a distance; God alone, whose essence is infinite, and who is all in all, can work in this way."

Therefore, if God works all in all by Himself without the aid of any created spirit or body, then the Devil is not the actor in the **Weapon Ointment**; rather, God works all things in all things through Himself, without the essential assistance or aid of any creature. Therefore, the Devil works nothing of or through himself (even though in his office and duty he is evil and destructive), much less does he work for the sake of doing good—as we assume happens through the healing from the weapon ointment. Such a thing is most contrary to the Devil's nature, as he was created and ordained (after his fall and defect) for a different purpose.

The major premise is clear, because the universal comprehends every particular case. And accordingly, if God works all things in all things, the Devil works nothing at all; and since healing is an operation, it is for that reason the work of God alone.

<margin>1 Corinthians 12.</margin>
<margin>Psalm 107:20.</margin>
<margin>Wisdom 16.</margin>
<margin>John 1.</margin>
The minor premise, or the assumption, is confirmed by these words of the Apostle: *There are diversities of gifts, but the same Spirit; and there are diversities of administrations, but one Lord; and there are diversities of operations, but one God, and the same one works all in all.* Through one and the same Spirit, the gifts of healing are given. From these words, it appears that God first works all in all through His spirit, and among these operations, that most noble act and gift of healing is numbered. Accordingly, it is not the Devil, but God alone who heals. Again, the Prophet says: *He sent His word and healed them.* And the Wise Man says: *Your word (O Lord) heals all things.* And Saint John: *In the word was life,* etc. For this reason, every healing and life-giving power descends from Him, as intended by the Speaker or Creator from the beginning, to give form, life, and existence to all things. In contrast, in the Devil there is death and destruction. For the Prophet testifies that he was created to destroy; therefore, nothing except affliction and wounding with disease, death, and destruction can be expected from him in his created nature—and this is particularly true after his fall.

But I know Mr. Foster will reply that it is indeed true that God is the primary and principal cause of all things; but that there are many subordinate efficient causes which work from themselves, and that according to their own inclination—some toward good, and others toward evil.

<margin>Isaiah 44:24.</margin>
<margin>Daniel 4.</margin>
To this I reply, it is admitted if he understands "instrumental causes" <note>original: "organicas causas" — causes that act like a tool in a hand, rather than having their own independent power.</note> and not "essential efficient causes"—such as Angels, stars, winds, Elements, meteors (or imperfect bodies), and perfect or composite natures. But it appears clearly that God alone works in them essentially and through all of them. I prove this with many passages of Holy Scripture, harmoniously agreeing in a single sense. For example: *I am the Lord* (says the Lord through the Prophet), *making all things alone, and none with me.* And again: *According to His will He does both among the powers of heaven and the inhabitants of the earth, nor is there any who can resist His hand.* By these it is clear that God alone, without any assisting creature, works essentially into any instrumental subject, as into a creaturely tool, to perform His will and pleasure through it, both in heaven and on earth; and without that act, the creature is like a trunk—

<sig>B 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: ci (likely continuing the word 'trunci' meaning 'of a trunk' or 'trunco' meaning 'trunk')</meta>

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that all healing, including that facilitated by the Weapon Ointment, is a direct gift of God. He uses Foster's own logic and Scriptural citations to argue that the Devil is a destroyer by nature and cannot be the source of a restorative, healing act.</summary>

# SPONGES FOR FOSTER.
<meta>Catchword: [it may] pro-</meta>

<margin>Isaiah 46:10.</margin>
...dead things, which can hardly do more than a flute without the blower’s breath; and indeed, regarding this effect, the Prophet speaks thus: "My counsel shall stand, and all my will shall be done," calling a bird of prey from the Eastern quarter of the world, and a man according to my will from a very distant region; I have spoken, and I will bring it to pass; I have created, and I will perform it. By these words it is clear that, just as the breath of His mouth—which He sent forth for the animation of every creature—moves that creature wherever the will of the Creator or Inspirer leads it, so the spirit of the creature, which shares in His power and will, immediately obeys. It carries its bodily instrument or organ along with it to fulfill the will of its Creator, which is <margin>Job 23:13.</margin> irresistible, according to that passage: "Whatever God has willed, He does."

And this operation of God, as much through Himself as through His created instruments, extends not only to common and manifest actions and effects, but also to mysterious and hidden ones—and even to the miraculous and astounding. Just so, this healing by the <term>Weapon Ointment</term> <note>original: "Unguentum Armarium" — a controversial medicinal salve applied to the weapon that caused a wound rather than the wound itself, believed to heal by "magnetic" sympathy.</note> appears to the fantasies and imaginations of worldly people, forcing them to marvel and stand stunned like a flock of small birds at the sight of an owl in an ivy bush. It provokes them in various ways according to the wisdom of this world, and according to each person's particular fancy. Some boldly and confidently exclaim that it is the work of the devil. Others determine it to be excessive nonsense and a vain imagination in the less-believing—though those who have a good opinion of it are healed. Still others condemn it as a superstitious and abominable method of healing, because the selection of ingredients must be made with astrological observation. And indeed, some, coming closer to the truth, call it <term>Natural Magic</term> <note>original: "Magia naturalis" — in this period, this referred to the study of the hidden, natural powers of the physical world (like magnetism), distinct from "black magic" involving demons.</note>, or a magnetic and secret act of nature. Yet others, more essentially rooted and religiously observing the prescribed order of the Holy Scriptures (as becomes true Christians), refer such a miraculous and astounding act of healing—as they would any other miraculous work—to that glorious God who created both heaven and earth. They believe He assigned to them, through His spirit, such virtues that work miraculously and astoundingly in the eyes of worldly men, as well as other virtues appearing more familiar to their senses, according to that saying of David: <margin>Psalm 33:6.</margin> "By the word of the Lord the heavens were made firm, and by the breath of His mouth all their power." And indeed, their principal foundation for establishing the right of God—and for abolishing and removing all such miraculous and astounding faculties or powers as are falsely ascribed to the devil by the blinded people of the world—is prescribed for them from the divine and truthful hymn of the Royal Prophet:

<margin>Psalm 72:18.</margin>
"Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, who alone and through Himself works wonders!" Or as it stands elsewhere:

<margin>Psalm 136:3-4.</margin>
> "Give thanks to the Lord, for His mercy endures forever; to Him who alone does great wonders."

And consequently, it is not any Devil, nor Angel, nor man, nor medicine, but God alone who performs this. Through this, He imparted the spiritual gift of healing to man and His creatures at their creation and has continued the same in them from generation to generation.

Therefore it is clear that God alone essentially works all things in all, and not any created instrument, whether it be spiritual or bodily—and consequently, in no way the Devil. (For the Devil is an instrument of darkness, destined and animated or driven only to produce the effects of darkness, such as diseases and destruction; he is not occupied with goodness, and especially not with the works of light, such as life, healing, and preservation.) It is as if one were to say that God—acting and working essentially in all and above all—did not create good Angels or spiritual instruments to bring into action and perfect the gifts of life and health which He, out of His mercy, imparted to His creatures; but instead, He must choose as His spiritual instrument to perform such beneficial effects one whom He created for an entirely contrary purpose—as will be proven immediately.

The divine mind and will cannot be resisted or obstructed, either in His creation or after it; that is, no effect can be produced contrary to His will and decree.

But God's intent and will was to appoint the devil as His instrument or minister for punishing and afflicting with diseases, infirmity, and death.

Therefore, this intent of His—both in the creation of the Devil and in his destination after his fall—cannot be obstructed or contradicted by any effect which is contrary to that prior divine will and decree.

<margin>Daniel 4:35.</margin>
Therefore, for greater confirmation, we find it written: "God does according to His will, as much with the heavenly virtues and powers as with the inhabitants of the earth, and no one exists who is able to resist His hand." And again: <margin>Job 23:13.</margin> "Whatever God has willed, He does." And again, regarding His will <margin>Isaiah 46:10.</margin> in creation (as before): "My counsel shall stand, and all my will shall be done." <margin>Genesis 50:19.</margin> And for that reason it stands written in Genesis: "Can we resist the will of God?" <margin>Romans 9:19.</margin> And the Apostle has: "Who may resist the will of God?" <margin>Proverbs 19:21.</margin> No creature; and the reason is given by the wise Solomon: because the will of the Lord shall remain forever. Through whom, then, I ask, does He work and bring into action this will and decree of His toward kindness and healing? What? Is it through the devil? Against his original destination? Indeed, through him who is rebellious against all goodness? Is it possible that he, who possesses not even a shred of integrity, could pro-
