Fol. 7.

### EXPRESSION OF MEMBER I.

B 3

<meta>Catchword: it may</meta>

...can it produce and bring into effect such a gift of goodness and charity as that of healing? Not at all; rather, this is perfected through Christ, the universal Savior, who is the head of all powers and potencies. He does not alter even a tiny detail in performing the will of God in heaven and on earth, as it is said:

<margin>John 6:38</margin>
"I came down from heaven, not to do my own will, but the will of him who sent me." Therefore, our Spiritual Lord Jesus is the only one whose power was given by the Father, who brings everything to its final effect—that which was decreed by the Father without any contradiction. This is certainly not done by false gods, nor by angels, nor by devils, nor by men, according to what I related earlier:

<margin>1 Corinthians 8:5-6</margin>
"Though there be those who are called gods, whether in heaven or on earth... yet for us there is but one God, the Father, from whom are all things; and one Christ Jesus, through whom are all things." By these words it is argued that God the Father decrees as the Parent and root of all things, in whom they existed implicitly and ideally before any beginning; but the Son essentially perfects His will, and makes every ideal appear explicitly and in reality. For this reason, neither angels, nor devils, nor stars, nor anything else—but only our Lord Jesus Christ, moving in His ministers both spiritual and bodily—brings about all things that exist, both within creation and beyond it, and consequently the art of healing.

<margin>Psalm 78:49</margin>
And from here we are taught by the Scriptures (as was said before) that it is the Word that heals all those painful effects of disease which the Devil brings into action. For first, the Psalmist says: "He cast upon them the fierceness of his anger, wrath, and indignation, and trouble, by sending evil angels among them." Thus, here one may see the effects of the office of the Devil and his angels: namely, to strike, to wound, and to afflict with diseases. And then afterward, in another place, he shows the immediate healer of such diabolical effects in these words:

<margin>Psalm 107:20</margin>
"He sent his word and healed them." And the wise man says: "Neither herb nor <term>plaster</term> <note>original: "Malagmate" — a soft, warming medical poultice or ointment used to soften tissues or reduce swelling.</note> cured them, but your word, O Lord, which healeth all things." Similarly, I say to you, by Hercules, it is not the herb, nor any animal or mineral medicine; but the gift of healing, assigned to the said creatures in their creation through the Word, which heals.

<margin>Isaiah 54:16</margin>
As for the <term>minor premise</term> <note>In logic, Fludd is building a syllogism to prove his point.</note>, that expression of the Prophet is confirmed, speaking in the person of God Himself: "Behold, I have created the smith that bloweth the coals in the fire, and that bringeth forth an instrument for his work; and I have created the waster to destroy." In these words one may see that the will of God was not that the Devil be created (or at least appointed after his fall) as a healer, preserver, or maker, but as a wounder and destroyer. Indeed, his nature was made so malignant and malicious that he not only envies the prosperity of man (and therefore, against the nature of the office assigned to him by God at creation or after his fall, he cannot be a healing angel), but he even feels ill and grieves at the excellence of God his Creator,

<margin>as in Wisdom 2</margin>
as appears in the third chapter of Genesis. Hence Solomon says: "Through the envy of the devil, death entered into the world." And therefore, how far he is from performing the office of healing or preservation! For this reason, Christ was sent to resist the evil acts of Satan:

<margin>Hebrews 2:14</margin>
"Christ by his death destroyed the devil, who had the power of death," says St. Paul. He is that health-giving emanation of God the Father of life, who from the very beginning was ordained to restrain and conquer the devil and his malicious actions and effects.

<margin>1 Peter 5:8</margin>
Inasmuch as "the devil your adversary walks about like a roaring lion, seeking whom he may devour." Has he then now turned into a physician and a <term>shape-shifter</term> <note>original: "versipellis" — literally "turn-skin," implying someone who changes their skin or character to deceive.</note> in that office for which he was created according to God's will? Did he kill so many men while he was a "trainee doctor," only to have discovered now, through long experience, the <term>Weapon Ointment</term> <note>The salve applied to the weapon that caused a wound.</note> to heal some of them? Truly, this is an outstanding and clever conclusion by Master Foster, and the nourishing father of the Jesuitical Johannes Robertus, in this blind imagination!

Now, I shall clothe myself with such audacity as to produce an argument for this ointment not at all unlike the one Master Foster fashioned.

If there is no institution or authority from the Sacred Page to certify any curative effect or craft of the devil, whether by supernatural or natural means, there is no reason why we should believe that healing performed by the Weapon Ointment is accomplished by the help of the devil.

But in the Sacred Page, no such certification is found for the healing of wounds by the devil, whether with the assistance of supernatural or natural means.

Therefore, it is not to be believed that healing by the Weapon Ointment can be accomplished by the art or act of the devil.

The <term>major premise</term> is of one and the same effect as that argument of Foster's, where he seems to assert that because the use of this weapon ointment is not established by divine institution, nor does it have any testimony or example from the Sacred Page to confirm it, it is therefore magical and delusory. And again (as was said before), what God originally decreed in the Archetypal Idea cannot be altered; but the devil was ordained for another end, entirely contrary to the pious and kindly act of healing.

<margin>Matthew 12:22</margin>
The <term>minor premise</term> is proved by what has already been said; just as the words of our Savior are extremely effective and do much for our purpose: for when He had healed someone who was both blind and mute and possessed by an evil spirit...

<summary>Fludd argues that healing is a divine gift from Christ and the Word, while the Devil's nature is strictly limited to destruction and death. He mocks his opponent, Foster, by using Foster's own logical methods to prove that the "Weapon Salve" cannot be diabolical since the Bible never grants the Devil the power to heal.</summary>

# FOSTER’S SPONGES.

<meta>catchword: may be witnessed [testetur]</meta>

...so that (as the Text says) he who was blind and mute could see and speak: the Pharisees replied: "this man casts out demons by Beelzebub, the prince of demons"; but Christ countered: "every kingdom divided against itself is brought to nothing, and every house or city divided against itself does not stand; so if Satan casts out Satan, he is divided against himself." From these things it is clear that all diseases, both internal and external, are inflicted upon men by God’s decree through the Devil and his Angels—namely, created ministers through whom and in whom God is accustomed to exercise vengeance and punish mortal creatures. It would be an unspeakable thing <note>original: "Nefas esset"</note> and no slight injury to the office assigned to them at their creation (or immediately after it) to exercise violence against their own kind by casting them out or healing the damage they themselves caused. Many passages are given in the sacred writings to confirm that Satan and his Angels are contrary and opposed to the healing art, and are always ready to bring harm through diseases and to afflict with sickness; but truly we cannot find any passage that may be witnessed <note>original: "testetur"</note> as assigning any healing faculty to him. For example, it is said that power was assigned to four Angels,
<margin>Revelation 7</margin>
who were created by God as rulers of the four winds to harm the earth, the sea, and the trees; now, each of these was a prince of many legions of malignant and wounding spirits. Again, the Prophet says: "God came from the South, the heavens were covered by his glory, and the earth was filled with his praise; at his feet was death" (as Jeremiah has it);
<margin>Habakkuk 3 <note>Source text says Aba. 33, likely referring to Habakkuk 3:5</note>. Psalm 91.</margin>
"the Devil shall go forth before his feet"; but other interpreters say that the plague preceded him, and a raging and destroying contagion in the South. And David says, "fear not the epidemic raging in the South" (as some interpreters have it), but Saint Jerome says, "from the noon-day devil" <note>original: "Dæmonio meridiano," a traditional personification of the plague or sudden midday illness</note>. We find it was Satan who was God’s instrument or organ by which He performed His will upon Job, and
<margin>Job 1</margin>
thence he says to the Creator: "put forth your hand upon him now," etc. By which words it can be signified that Satan himself attributed more reverence to his Creator in this acknowledgment and confession than Dr. Foster himself, who makes this organ of disease a peremptory and absolute agent, as much in the effect of healing as of destruction—as if it were of his own self, and not merely as an organ and instrument through which God essentially works His own ends of vengeance against those who offend Him. This, therefore, was he who wounded that patient Job with a foul ulcer or tumor, and stirred up a burning fever in his spirits, which forced him to cry out in his torments:
<margin>Job 6</margin>
"The arrows of the Almighty are bitter against me, their poison drinks up my spirit, and the tribulations of God, which sharply withstand me, set themselves in array against me." Here he attributes everything to God, and not to Satan, who is merely His wounding organ. Similarly, David beheld the Angel of the Lord,
<margin>1 Chronicles 21</margin>
the destroyer between heaven and earth, with a drawn sword in his hand stretched out toward Jerusalem, who struck down a great number with the plague. In like manner, Joshua <note>The text likely means Hezekiah/Isaiah, as noted in the margin reference to 2 Chronicles 32:21</note>
<margin>2 Chronicles 32:21</margin>
sent forth the killing Angel to the Camp of the Assyrians, who slew every brave and strong man-at-arms in the space of one night. Thus Moses, using a destructive organ of God (using, in place of the <term>weapon salve</term> <note>original: "unguenti armarij," a controversial remedy where ointment was applied to the weapon that caused the wound rather than the wound itself</note>, which was contrary to his office, art, and industry, the sprinkling of a certain infectious and contagious powder), afflicted the Egyptians with ulcers and pustules. But that it is not the Angelic organ, but God in the organ Himself who essentially acts and strikes, appears from this passage where Jehovah says: "About midnight I will go out into the
<margin>Exodus 9</margin>
<margin>Exodus 11:5</margin>
<margin>Exodus 12:13</margin>
midst of Egypt and all the firstborn shall die." But in the chapter immediately following it is said: "Jehovah will pass over that door and will not suffer the destroyer or striker to devastate your houses." From this it is clear that the essential act of God is present with the actual destroyer and acts within him. Finally, to demonstrate to you that there were malignant spirits who, by the movement of their Creator, caused and produced such diseases in Egypt: the Royal Prophet <note>David</note> argues in those words cited before, "He cast upon them the fierceness of His anger, wrath, and tribulation, by sending evil angels." Indeed, I can prove this with many examples from the sacred Text: namely, how God punishes and afflicts through diseases, infirmities, and death, through such ministers of His—the destroyers or organic causes—whom He created by design to perform and fulfill His will in this manner of affliction. But it cannot be proved that He employed them in the opposite office, namely, in that of curing and healing; for when it pleases Him to heal, He has an infinity of good Angels to perform that duty for Him, all of whom are included under the dominion of the Archangel Raphael, who for this reason holds the name meaning <term>Medicine of God</term> <note>original: "Medicina Dei"</note>.

I must conclude, therefore, that just as it is a single act of God both to wound and to heal, so He has ordained instruments or organs to serve Him in the execution of both types of operations, which are as contrary to one another as Light is to darkness and good to evil. Now, that it is the sole and unique act of God both to heal through His healthy organs and to strike and wound through His destructive organs, we are taught from various passages of Holy Scripture. "If Jehovah shall strike Egypt with a plague, He who struck with plagues shall heal, when they are turned back to Jehovah"; yet David confesses,

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that while God uses "evil angels" as instruments of disease and vengeance, these entities are incapable of healing. He concludes that healing—such as that attributed to the "Weapon Salve"—is a divine act performed through "good" instruments, as the roles of destroyer and healer are fundamentally separate.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s refutation of William Foster. It completes the sentence from the previous page, which argued that while the Devil may be the "hand" that strikes, the ultimate power over life, death, and healing belongs solely to God. Fludd transitions from a purely theological argument based on scripture to a "Theophilosophical" argument based on the hidden workings of nature.</meta>

# EXPLANATION OF THE FIRST PART

<margin>Job 5</margin>
as witnessed <note>original: "testetur"</note>, that He afflicted them through evil Angels; and Job says: It is God who wounds and heals, strikes and makes whole; and yet it was Satan who was the hand of the Lord to actuate and exacerbate Job’s misery.
<margin>Deuteronomy 32</margin>
And elsewhere: I will kill and I will make alive, I will strike and I will heal, and there is no one who can rescue from my hand.
<margin>Wisdom 16:13</margin>
And Solomon: You have power over life and death, You lead down to the gates of hell and bring back again, and nothing can escape Your hand. From these passages it is clear that it is God alone who—either miraculously or by the virtue and assistance of His creatures, which He ordained for this or that health-giving purpose—cures and wounds. He does this again by the means of His organic ministers, whether they be Angelic or human. For example: He makes His Angel Raphael use a fish’s liver and gall for the purpose of driving away a destroying Demon and for curing the blind Tobias. Similarly, an Angel gave healing power to the pool of Bethesda.
<margin>Psalm 91</margin>
And again, the Psalmist says: "no plague shall come near your tent, for He will give His Angels charge over you." But if I could find anywhere in the scriptures, by any authority of the sacred Text, that He used Satan or any of his Angels of Darkness to complete and perform any such act of light, or to make use of any creature of God—such as fat, blood, or flesh—in the healing of a wounded creature, I would in some way give it credit. But because this would involve Mr. Foster in too lengthy an inquiry, and again, because I find the preceding passages of scripture generally contradict him, I conclude that the mystical healing by the <term>weapon-salve</term> <note>original: "unguentum armarium," a medicinal ointment applied to the weapon that caused a wound rather than the wound itself</note> is a beneficial gift of God alone. Therefore, let all virtue, power, and glory for His beneficence in general, and particularly for this power and property of healing through the weapon-salve, be attributed to Him alone in every way, and not to any Devil, forever and ever, Amen.
<margin>Psalm 71</margin>
I say with the Prophet David: Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, who alone works all miracles, and therefore effects this marvelous way of healing, which surpasses human understanding; therefore, let honor and glory for it be attributed to Him alone forever and ever.

## CHAPTER III.

->*How, by a certain hidden inquiry made into the mysteries of Sacred*<-
->*Philosophy, the question proposed concerning the lawful or unlawful*<-
->*use of the weapon-salve is resolved, and Foster’s*<-
->*sponge is thoroughly squeezed and*<-
->*wrung out.*<-

<margin>Colossians 2:9</margin>
In this preceding chapter, I have proved theologically, and that by pure examples from scripture, that the devil was a spiritual instrument ordained and established by God at creation for a purpose entirely contrary and opposite to that of healing. Therefore, it is hardly likely that now, at the end of days, after so many destructions committed and intended by him in his past age, he could turn into a Savior and healer of diseases.

Now, however, I will explain and confirm more clearly through <term>Sacred Philosophy</term> <note>original: "Philosophiam sacram"</note>—the life-giving spirit of God moving in the aerial instrument of the world—how He perfects the cure of the wounded by His virtuous application and influence. I call this method of proof <term>Theophilosophical</term> <note>original: "Theophilosophicum," meaning a philosophy rooted in the divine</note>, or pertaining to sacred Philosophy, because it regards the nature of this mode of healing insofar as it receives its essential act and being from God, who moves and acts in and through created spiritual instruments, in and over all things. Of this species of Philosophy, Saint Paul speaks in this manner: "See that no one deceives you through philosophy and empty deceit, according to the tradition of men, according to the elements of the world, and not according to Christ Jesus; for in Him dwells all the fullness of the Godhead bodily, and you are filled in Him, who is the head of all principality and power." By these words, he seems to call Ethnic <note>meaning "Pagan"</note> Philosophy vain and deceptive insofar as it is fashioned according to the rudiments of this world, which establish many essential subordinate agents that act and work absolutely and simply of themselves, without any consideration for the Universal <note>original: "Catholicam"</note> and supreme power of God the Father of all. From Him all essential actions radically proceed, which are afterwards produced through our Lord Jesus Christ, through whom all things have their existence—and not, in truth, from any subordinate agent or performing creature. For although they serve like organic ministers, they are nonetheless dead unless God appears and works in them through His spirit. And therefore every act and operation is attributed to God, as was proved before by the Apostle saying:
<margin>1 Corinthians 12</margin>
"One God works all in all"; and again he says:
<margin>1 Corinthians 8</margin>
"Although there are those who are called Gods in heaven and on earth, yet for us there is only one God, who is the Father, from whom are all things and we in Him; and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom are all things and we through Him." And for this reason, God is called the Alpha and Ome-
<margin>Revelation 1</margin>

<sig>B 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: ga, prin- [completing "Omega, principle"]</meta>

<summary>Fludd concludes his theological argument that only God (and not the Devil) has the power to heal, specifically defending the "weapon-salve." He then introduces a "Theophilosophical" approach, arguing that all natural healing is the work of God acting through spiritual and physical instruments.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s theological defense of the "Weapon Salve." He is concluding a sentence from the previous page regarding God as the "Alpha and Omega," the beginning and the end of all natural actions. He moves from pure theology into "Theosophy"—a blend of biblical study and natural philosophy—to explain how a medicinal ointment can heal a wound from a distance.</meta>

# FOSTER'S SPONGE
<meta>catchword: It is evident</meta>

<margin>Wisdom 8:1</margin>
...mega, <note>Completing the thought from the previous page: "Alpha and Omega."</note> the beginning and the end; namely, insofar as He decrees all things within Himself, and essentially works and effects all things through His word, and does so immediately. Now, the Wise Man expressly suggests that this very spirit is in all things, saying: "The incorruptible spirit of God is in all and in everything," and that from the spirit of the Divine mouth proceed <margin>Psalm 33:6</margin> the virtues <note>original: "virtutes," referring here to the inherent powers or "strengths" of natural objects.</note> of all things, as the Prophet David asserts. Since this is the case, it necessarily follows that the essential form or life of any creature in this world must depend upon this spirit, and must possess its central seat of activity in a greater or lesser proportion from this "Agent of agents." For, just as He acts in the center of all things, so He is in truth touching the circumference, from Alpha (the Center) to Omega (the circumference). Otherwise, there would be a division of the divine essence, which is impossible. For this reason, God is said to fill all things and to work all things in everyone. Therefore, the Apostle <note>Referring to St. Paul.</note> concludes, as before, that "Ethnic" <note>i.e., Pagan or non-Christian.</note> philosophy, fashioned from the traditions and inventions of human wisdom, is false and deceitful; but that philosophy which is founded upon Christ alone must be embraced alone, because in Him dwells the fullness of divinity, and because He works all in all according to the will and decree of His Father, who is the head of the Powers and Principalities, and consequently of all Angels. Thus, the Angels can work nothing except as He Himself acts in them and through them, as His own spiritual organs. Behold, therefore, that **Theosophical subject** <note>The "subject" refers to the Divine Spirit acting as the medium for healing.</note> through which I strictly inquire into the mystery of this mode of healing by the **Weapon Salve** <term>Weapon Salve: Also called the "armary ointment," this was a controversial 17th-century remedy applied to the weapon that caused a wound, rather than the wound itself, believed to heal by "sympathy."</term>. Let these things suffice as a preamble to our discourse. Let us come to the matter itself.

We must observe three principal things in the cure performed by this Weapon Salve: namely, the **Wounded Man**, the **healing or cure of the Ointment**, and finally the **occult activity** which rules in the blood and proceeds from the blood to the Ointment.

The Wounded Man may not undeservedly or inconveniently be compared to the world, and for that reason is called the "lesser world" <term>Microcosm: The ancient and Renaissance idea that a human being is a miniature map of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</term>. He is composed of heaven and earth—namely, of spirit and body. Just as the Creator sent out His spirit, which moved over the waters and informed, animated, and gave life to them—so that from them and through them (as Saint Peter says) the heaven and earth were formed and established by this spiritual word until this very day—in exactly the same way, the "heaven and earth" of man were formed by the same eternal spirit of life, upon which he rests and endures in his specific succession to this very day. <margin>Isaiah 51, Acts 17, 1 Corinthians 3</margin> Therefore it is said: "You were made from one and the same spiritual rock," and that "in Him we live, move, and have our being," and that we are <margin>1 Corinthians 8, Luke 17:21, John 1</margin> **Temples of the Holy Spirit** and members of Christ; and that we are in God the Father and through our Lord Jesus Christ (as cited before); and that the "kingdom of God is within us." Nor should we ascribe this beneficence of God only to ourselves, for it extends beyond us to every creature, though not as abundantly. For, "in the Word was life"; and Judith says in her prayer: <margin>Judith 16</margin> "Let every creature serve God, because You spoke and they were made; You sent Your spirit and they were created." <margin>Isaiah 42:5</margin> And the Prophet says: "Thus says God who creates the heavens and stretches them out, who spreads out the earth and what sprouts from it, who gives breath to the people upon it and spirit to those who walk on it." <margin>Psalm 104</margin> And King David: "When God gives to the creatures, they gather it; when He hides His face, they are troubled; when He takes away their spirit, they die; when He sends forth His spirit, they are renewed." <margin>Job 34:13</margin> And Job: "God placed man upon the earth, setting His soul near him; if He should take back His spirit or breath to Himself, all flesh would fail and die, and man would return to ashes." By all these testimonies we teach that the life, form, and nature of every creature essentially arises and proceeds from God. Because of this, whatever gift of healing is found to proceed either from compound or simple medicines—be they angelic, celestial, or elemental, or from animal, vegetable, or mineral compositions—it proceeds from their Creator. It was either imparted to them at their creation for this health-giving purpose, or otherwise miraculously granted by nature to certain creatures to act beyond the common laws of nature. Therefore, it is not right to rashly condemn or reject a medicine simply because it works in a manner different from others. For God has bestowed and granted to certain medicines **occult and hidden properties**, and for that reason, they do not work through an external or manifest "Elemental" quality <note>In Fludd's time, "elemental qualities" were heat, cold, moisture, and dryness. "Occult" properties were powers that couldn't be explained by these four basics.</note>. <margin>Reuchlin, *On the Art of the Kabbalah*</margin> This hidden force is called by some an "Angelic act," which is a middle virtue between God and nature, from which operations are performed in things that their own nature would either not do, or would do differently. Some say these come from an "occult property" and others "because they are such." And such indeed was the effect of healing by the water of the pool of **Bethesda**; it was not that the manifest Elemental quality of the water performed it, but the act of the Angel stirring the water. In a similar way, no one can express a manifest natural reason for the attraction of iron by a **Magnet**, or straw by **Amber** <note>original: "Caraben"</note>, or why the Magnet looks toward the North, or why the Laurel Tree preserves one from the harm of lightning and thunder; and similarly, how this cure is performed across a straight line <note>Referring to the "line of sight" or distance over which the Weapon Salve was said to work.</note>, etc. The causes of these things are latent and hidden from common philosophers—but let me come a little closer to the point.

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that all life and healing power stem from a single Divine Spirit that permeates the entire universe. He defends the Weapon Salve by comparing its "occult" (hidden) action to other natural mysteries like magnetism and the medicinal properties of plants, which operate beyond simple physical explanations.</summary>
<keywords>Weapon Salve, Microcosm, Occult Properties, Magnetism, Bethesda, Divine Spirit, Robert Fludd, Theosophy, Healing, Natural Philosophy</keywords>

### EXPRESSION OF THE FIRST MEMBER. Fol. 9.
C
<meta>catchword: ...rum</note>

<margin>Wisdom 1</margin>
It is evident, therefore, that the incorruptible spirit is in all things, but it exists most abundantly (next after the macrocosm <note>The "macrocosm" refers to the universe or "great world."</note>) in the microcosm, which is to say, in man. For, as God is truly said—according to the translation of Saint Jerome (setting aside the corruption of others)—to have placed his tabernacle in the sun, from which He emits life and multiplication to every member and creature of the greater world through a perpetual and never-ending motion and through the agility of His Spirit. For Solomon says:
<margin>Wisdom 7</margin>
*It is more mobile than any other thing:* It moves and provides life to the entire worldly Spirit, which the wise man also testifies to in these words:
<margin>Ecclesiastes 1</margin>
*The sun, circling from the south, turns toward the North; surveying all things, the spirit proceeds in its circuit.* By these words, he proves that the Spirit in the sun animates and provides motion, life, and Spirit to the aerial Spirit of the whole world. For without its constant motion and action—as Aristotle and all other Philosophers confess—the air would quickly become corrupted and would appear as if dead, possessing no efficacy at all. Following the logic of this sacred Text, he concludes that the Holy Spirit of Discipline fills the whole world. In the same way, that same incorruptible Spirit fills the Microcosm <note>The "microcosm" refers to the human being as a "little world" mirroring the universe.</note>
<margin>1 Corinthians 6</margin>
(for man is the temple of the Holy Spirit) and has placed His Tabernacle in the heart of man. In the heart, He moves just as He does in this Macrocosmic sun, through *systole and diastole* <note>The contraction and dilation of the heart.</note>—that is, through contraction and expansion—unceasingly dispersing the rays of His life through the entire human form to illuminate it, providing life and circular motion to man's Spirit. Because of this, the Apostle <note>St. Paul</note> speaks reciprocally of this lesser world as he does elsewhere of the greater: "In God," he says, "we live, move, and have our being." Just as that hidden spirit gives heat through its activity and essential motion to the greater world and all remaining things, since they do not rest or hide within themselves, as will be explained shortly. This being well-considered, I proceed in the following manner.

Just as this Principal and central motor acts radically in the spirit of both worlds and moves essentially in and over all things—namely, from the Center toward the circumference—its first "mobile" <note>A "primum mobile" or "first moved thing" in ancient physics.</note> in the greater world is the principal Ethereal region, or sphere. By its revolution, the sun (which, as David holds, is a vessel full of the glory of God) is moved around the earth within 24 hours, so that from there the whole Spirit of the world is restored by life, growth, and multiplication. Consequently, the first and noblest sphere of this spirit, in which it moves centrally, is the quintessential or Ethereal spirit of life—the immediate vehicle, so to speak, of that incorruptible spirit of life. It is carried into the thicker Elemental or sublunary air, by which medium it penetrates into the bodies of animals, vegetables, and minerals through inspiration or expiration. In animals, this is partly hidden, such as through the pores of the body, and partly manifest, as through the lungs. In vegetables and minerals, it is hidden, so that it is perceived only by the "eyes of the mind." In this way, it bestows and imparts life and multiplication to every single thing. As this emanation proceeded from God into the world, the Prophet says:
<margin>Psalm 104:2</margin>
*He is clothed in light as if with a garment:* And so,
<margin>Psalm 33:6</margin>
*By the Word of the Lord the heavens were made, and all their power by the Spirit from his mouth.* Among these "powers," life, form, growth, and multiplication were the chief ones. As the Ethereal or starry heaven took possession of itself, the same Prophet says:
<margin>Psalm 18</margin>
*He set His Tabernacle in the sun.* Again, as He put on a thicker garment made of air, the Prophet says:
<margin>Psalm 104</margin>
*The thick cloud is His dwelling, who rides upon the wings of the wind.* Again, He spoke in thunder, and lightning flashed from His nostrils:
<margin>2 Samuel 22</margin>
As He entered into the lesser world or man, the Apostle says: *You are the Temple of the Holy Spirit; you are members of Christ.*

And again:
<margin>Isaiah 45:8</margin>
*Let the earth be opened, and let it bring forth a Savior;* as it penetrated into the earth, the wise man says:
<margin>Wisdom 1</margin>
*The spirit of wisdom fills the orb of the earth.* Finally, so that He might create, give life to, and sustain every creature, He "put on" all things; thus it is said:
<margin>Wisdom 12</margin>
*The incorruptible spirit is in all things,* and again: *The Spirit of God fills all things.* By these it is clear that this divine and incorruptible spirit, through which we live, move, and have our being, is in man—for without it, man is dead, a mere bubble, or nothing. Therefore, the place in which it moves is our ether or celestial Spirit, which acts invisibly in our aerial vehicle, the thicker and denser part of which is the blood—both the vital and arterial, as well as the natural and venous.

Hence those specific statutes or legal precepts handed down by God concerning the blood, not only of man but also of beasts: For, insofar as it was the seat of the spirit of life;
<margin>Genesis 9</margin>
*The blood (says God) which is the seat of your souls, I will require:* and again; *Whosoever sheds the blood of man, by man shall his blood be shed, for in His own image God made man.* From these it is gathered that, by reason of the divine Spirit dwelling in the human blood—through which we are formed in the image of God—God Himself gave a specific edict that we should have respect for the blood. For this reason, therefore,
<margin>Genesis 9</margin>
the voice of Abel's blood cried out for vengeance against the murderer Cain.
<margin>Leviticus 3</margin>
Indeed, the blood was so precious, even that of common animals or irrational creatures, that their blood was forbidden to be eaten with the...
<margin>Leviticus 17</margin>

<summary>This page argues that the "incorruptible spirit" of God acts as a central motor in both the universe (Macrocosm) and man (Microcosm), establishing the blood as the sacred physical vehicle for this divine life-force. Fludd uses biblical citations to prove that the sun powers the world's air just as the heart powers the body's blood, justifying the spiritual importance of blood in divine law.</summary>

# FOSTERIAN SPONGES.

...with their flesh. And again: *Whoever eats the blood of a creature shall die by death:* and elsewhere he reveals the reason: *Whoever eats blood, I will set my face against that soul, because the soul of the flesh is in the blood, and I have given it to you so that you may make atonement for your souls upon the altar, and the blood may serve as an expiation for the soul.*

<margin>Leviticus 17</margin>
<margin>Leviticus 17</margin>
And for this reason, it is commanded to man by God that if, in hunting or otherwise, he kills a wild beast, he must pour its blood upon the earth: and again, *you shall not take the blood of any animal as food.* Likewise:
<margin>Chronicles 9 <note>Original: "Chron. 9" Likely a reference to Genesis 9:4.</note></margin>
<margin>Chronicles 9</margin>
->Blood must not be eaten with the flesh.<-
<margin>Leviticus 3</margin>
->The blood of man is even required from the beast.<-
<margin>Leviticus 17, 19</margin>
->You shall not eat blood and fat at all.<-
<margin>Deuteronomy 12</margin>
->No soul among you shall eat blood.<-
<margin>Deuteronomy 15</margin>

*The blood of every animal, whether clean or unclean, you shall not eat, but you shall pour it out upon the earth like water.*

<margin>Chronicles 9:5 <note>Original: "Chron. 9. 5." Likely referring to Genesis 9:5.</note></margin>
And the reason is that the blood is the seat of the soul or vital spirit, which is inspired by God: and therefore it is said:
*I will require the blood, the seat of your souls:* Inasmuch as it is the seat of your life and soul: as if He were saying, *I have animated the internal spirit of your blood with my spirit of life: therefore, take care of it.*

From all these places, it is very easy to discern how the vital spirit of man—and not only of man, but also of the beast—is contained in that red vehicle of the blood, just as the Ethereal spirit is in the aerial one; and that the essential mover or steersman at the helm in this spiritual boat of man is the incorruptible spirit of God, *through whom we live, move, and have our being.* <margin>Pymander 12 <note>Pymander is the first book of the *Corpus Hermeticum*, a collection of Greco-Egyptian wisdom texts attributed to Hermes Trismegistus.</note></margin> The divine philosopher Hermes Trismegistus seems to confirm this whole matter with these words: *The soul of man is carried in this manner: the mind in the soul; the soul in the spirit; the spirit in the body; the spirit, diffused through the veins, arteries, and blood, moves the animal in every part.*

With these things thus evidently confirmed from the sacred page, we shall now proceed to a greater mystery regarding the twofold property of this Spirit of life in both worlds, so that from there we may gradually arrive demonstratively at the very point or most perfect resolution of the proposed question.

The eternal Lord God (who is entirely one and the same spirit due to His indivisible essence) is He Himself who gives life to creatures, and in turn takes life away from them according to His pleasure: as has already been proven before, and for that reason He *works all things in all,* according to the words of the Apostle. Truly, do not wonder if I say He works contrary effects, even though He is only one indivisible essence: for these are the words of Solomon: <margin>Wisdom 7</margin> *The Holy Spirit of discipline is unique (or simple), yet manifold, etc.* Simple in Himself, but manifold in operation. And does not David acknowledge as much, saying that when God sends forth His Spirit, <margin>Psalm 104</margin> creatures are recreated; when He hides His face, they are troubled; when He takes back their spirit, they expire, etc. As for the next part of this axiom of David, he proves it elsewhere in this manner: *Life depends <margin>Psalm 30, Psalm 41:3, Psalm 87:7, Psalm 90:17</margin> upon the benevolence of Jehovah: Jehovah is the restorer of life: all the fountains of my life are from God: the prolongation of life is the kindness of Jehovah.* From which it is evident that the spirit of God is the immediate Creator, actor, preserver, and multiplier of life.

<margin>Deuteronomy 3:17</margin>
As for the second part, we shall only offer this: *God left the wicked, and hid His face from the impious, so that many evils and anguishes might befall them.* Thus He is accustomed to rebuke mortals upon their beds, as Job has it. In this manner, He sent tribulations upon the <margin>Exodus 9</margin> Egyptians. In this way, He caused the hand of Jeroboam to wither; thus He struck <margin>2 Kings 4, Numbers 12</margin> Miriam, the sister of Aaron, with leprosy. Thus He afflicted the Ashdodites <margin>1 Samuel 6</margin> with hemorrhoids; thus He brought a plague upon Hezekiah <margin>2 Kings 20</margin>—namely, by hiding or withholding His Spirit. And again, by emitting the rays of life, He healed him. And therefore David says: *The life of man is a miserable span of time without the kindness of Jehovah:* For he who is sick always seems to be at the point of expiring.

I now come to the final clause of the aforementioned text of David: God said, <margin>Genesis 6:3, Genesis 34:13</margin> when He perceived the wickedness of man: *My Spirit shall not remain in man forever, because he is flesh, and his days shall be one hundred and twenty years.*

And Job says, as before: <margin>Job 5 <note>Reference to Job 34:14-15.</note></margin> *God placed man upon the earth, setting His soul upon him; if He should take back or withdraw His Spirit or breath to Himself, all flesh would fail and expire, and man would return to ashes at the same time.* And again: *The Spirit of God made me, and the inspiration of the Almighty gave me life.*

For this reason, therefore, the aforementioned action of David shall be the principal foundation upon which I shall rest and be established, both in my proofs and in demonstrating the various properties of this divine and incorruptible essence in the spirit of both worlds; and I shall evidently explain and prove to you that just as the spirit works in the macrocosm <note>The "great world" or the universe.</note>, so also in every respect it produces similar effects in that microcosm <note>The "little world" or the human being.</note>.

We have discovered that there is only one spirit in the macrocosm, although in its property...

Fludd asserts that God directly manages life and health by either emitting or withholding this divine Spirit within both the universe and the human body.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense against William Foster, moving from the presence of the soul in the blood to the presence of the Divine Spirit in the winds and the "Spirit of the World." # EXPRESSION OF MEMBER I. Leaf 16.
C 2
<meta>catchword: hemen-</meta>

<margin>Psalm 147.</margin>
...opposites, in two ways he animates the four winds sent from the four corners of the earth to stir up blasts; and I prove it thus: *God gives ice by his breath; by the breath of God the frost is formed, and the surface of the waters is narrowed* <note>original: "coarctatur," meaning compressed or frozen</note>. And the royal Prophet <note>King David</note> speaks even more to the point; *When God sends his speech,* he says, *to the earth, his word runs most swiftly: He who scatters snow like wool, and frost like ashes; he casts down his ice like morsels; who can stand before his cold? He sends out his word and melts them; as soon as he blows his wind, the waters flow.* From this it is clear that the Divine Spirit is the essential actor in the northern blast, which is an enemy to the act of life: For, just as God sent out the rays of his light from the infinite fountain of his essence to ward off cold through the expansion <term>dilatation|the spreading out of a substance</term> of spirits and to produce warm moisture in the spirit of the world—so that he might activate it with life and motion, making those spirits fluid and active which were previously frozen by the power of his contractive property (which is opposite to the other)—so, in turn, through a privative agent or his Northern <note>Boreal</note> attribute and property, which is cold, he contracts the expanded spirits. He makes them fixed instead of mobile, dark and opaque instead of bright and transparent; he turns living spirits into substances void of life, and liquid or fluid things into the immobile and frozen. Finally, motion is turned into rest, action into potential, and presence <note>original: "positio"</note> into deprivation. But on the other hand, when he intends to bring life back—to cause the dead spirits of the world to rise and live again—he sends out the southern spirit or blast of an entirely opposite property, namely a wind whose nature is warm and moist. And therefore, in the conclusion of the aforementioned Text it is said: *He sends out his word and melts all these things:* As soon as he blows his wind, the waters and spirits, which were dead, thick, fixed, frozen, and opaque, are given life, and they become mobile, fluid, subtle, and diaphanous or transparent.

But if none of these testimonies please Mr. Foster, I shall proceed a little further, providing an ocular demonstration to prove it, so that I might more easily persuade him, if he is willing to be managed by experience, which is the *mother of fools*: <note>original: "mater fatuorum est"</note> Let him only look into a weather glass <note>original: "vitrum Calendarium," an early thermoscope</note> (an instrument known to the English public as a motion-glass, and called by others an air-glass) and he will see the air contained within it contract and thicken with cold. To prove this, he will find that the water is drawn upward in the neck of the glass by as many degrees as the external cold dominates the air: This evidently proves that cold contracts the expanded air in the glass from a larger space into a narrower one; and consequently, the air is thicker, and closer to freezing, fixation, and rest than it was before. On the contrary, he will discover that the enclosed air, feeling the heat of the external air, gradually gains dominance over the cold, expanding itself; and by this expansion, it is made more mobile, subtle, and lively. Therefore, it requires a larger capacity, as is proven to the eye in this manner: namely, because the water is pushed downward by as many degrees as the external heat overcomes the cold in the air. 

But perhaps our antagonist will say: "What business is this of ours? These are natural conclusions, not pertaining to the act or property of God. And how do you prove that the other winds are essential acts of God?" To this I reply: if God works all things in all, then this operation in the glass—and much more that in the winds—is the act of God. For we find that the incorruptible spirit moves and works in two contrary ways: namely, from the center to the circumference by emanation and expansion, by which means he moves from himself, who is the center of all, whose circumference is nowhere; yet he encompasses all circumferences and bodies, and makes them exist and live. So also this universal center, contracting itself within itself, makes all things that are in action become merely potential; from a living creature, a dead corpse; from an agile and mobile thing, a dull and immobile one; and finally, from some actual positive thing, a potential, privative nothing. We clearly see this perfected (according to the preceding Text of David) in the Spirit of the World, insofar as it is altered from one form into its opposite according to the variety of the property or will of Him who is the internal agent of the world. <margin>Wisdom 12:1</margin> *The incorruptible Spirit of God,* says Solomon, *is in all things;* if it is in all things, then as the most worthy, most mobile, and most operative of all Spirits, it works centrally, and moves all the external wheels of the whole machine of creation in which it resides; and consequently, it works from the center to the circumference. If it should rest in the center, all the external wheels lose their life: For *in him it lives, in him it moves, in him it exists.* <note>Reference to Acts 17:28</note> And therefore, unless He acts, all things are dull and dead like mute stones, as David and Job have taught us. Now, to prove that it is this Spirit of God that agitates and animates the winds, we have many other express texts of scripture to confirm it: <margin>Job 36:9</margin> *The whirlwind,* says Job, *comes from the South,* and <margin>Numbers 11</margin> *cold from the North: by the spirit of God frost is given, and the spirit of the waters is narrowed.* And again: *A wind went forth from Jehovah and blew quails from the sea to the camp.* <margin>Exodus 15:10</margin> Again: *You blew with your wind, and the sea covered them; by the blast of your nostrils* <margin>Exodus 18:8</margin> *the waters were gathered together.* And again: *Jehovah* <margin>Exodus 10</margin> *brought an East wind.* <margin>Exodus 10</margin> Again: *A West wind...*

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that God’s spirit directly controls the physical world, specifically the winds, using a combination of Biblical citations and a demonstration of the "weather glass" (thermoscope) to show how heat expands and cold contracts the "Spirit of the World." He refutes his opponent, Mr. Foster, by asserting that all natural motion is the internal action of the Divine Spirit.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s argument regarding the Divine Spirit's role in the movement of the elements. Fludd is responding to William Foster's critique, specifically addressing how the "Spirit of the World" behaves like a breath that can either freeze or vivify life, bridging the gap between the vast universe (Macrocosm) and the individual human (Microcosm).</meta>

# FOSTER’S SPONGES

<margin>Psalm 144.</margin> ...sent it forth vehemently, etc. And again: *The stormy wind fulfilling the word of God.* We do not say that the wind is the actual Spirit of God, but rather a created spirit or air, animated by the uncreated spirit of the Lord. According to His will, He who sends it out sometimes withdraws its power into Himself from the outer edge <note>original: "circumferentia"</note> of the creaturely spirit. At that moment, the creature is dead, motionless, and rigid because it lacks the warm and fertile action of this spirit’s outflow from the center to the edges. This follows the preceding text of David: *When God hides his face from his creatures, they are troubled; when he takes away their breath, they die.* That is to say, if He partially withdraws Himself or hides the life-giving rays of His countenance, they become sick and troubled; but if He completely removes the spirit of life from the outer edges of the creature's spirit back into Himself—He who is in the center and everywhere at the edges—they die and pass away.

Mr. Foster might perhaps reply: "What does all this have to do with man, who is the primary subject we have undertaken to discuss? Or what does it have to do with me, even if God works a private property in the world’s spirit through His own spirit—by freezing it, or by pulling it from the spirit's edges to its center, leaving the spirit icy, devoid of heat, frozen, immobile, and as if dead or without life, especially in this northern nature of ours? What is this to our purpose? Or what is this to the nature of man? Or how can it touch the act of healing in our weapon-salve <term>unguentum armarium|a medicinal ointment believed to heal wounds by being applied to the weapon that caused them</term>?"

I answer that this very spirit is the cause of the fourfold nature in the spirit of the Macrocosm <term>Macrocosmic spirit|the spirit of the great world or the universe</term>. Just as it causes death and deprivation through its northern and freezing blast, and conversely causes life and presence through the opposite or softening nature of this southern property, it performs the same operation in the created spirit of the Microcosm <term>Microcosm|the 'little world,' referring to man as a reflection of the universe</term>. For that same spirit which gives life to the air in the greater world in four ways produces those same effects in the lesser world, which I prove in this manner.

<margin>Ezekiel 37:9.</margin> The Prophet says: *Come from the four winds, O Spirit, and breathe upon these slain, that they may live.* And the Spirit returned into them, and they lived, and stood upon their feet.

From these words, four things may be gathered. First, that there was only one single Spirit, which was endowed with the property of the four winds. Depending on the variety of its properties, the four winds were animated with contrary natures so that they might work in a fourfold way in the universal <note>original: "catholico"</note> element of the world, to fulfill the Creator's will in any manner. Furthermore, this <margin>2 Corinthians</margin> spirit, which is the essential actor and mover in the winds, was the incorruptible Spirit of the Lord, through whom He was accustomed (according to the Apostle) to give life to all things. And according to the <margin>Judith 16.</margin> Prophet, He gives breath to the people, and Spirit to those who tread the earth. Or, according to the book of <margin>1 Esdras 16</margin> <margin>Job 34:2.</margin> Job, He bestows His own soul upon man and all creatures. Or according to Esdras, by His breath He makes all things and searches out all things in the hidden places of the earth. Or, as Job says, He bestows His soul upon man and every creature: for if He should take back or draw His spirit or breath to Himself, all flesh would perish. And therefore, in this place, when He wished those corpses to rise again, He commanded the Prophet to say: *Come, spirit, from the four winds.* This is as if He had said: "Come, O universal spirit of the world's life, and perform your duty in reviving and animating these dead." Thirdly, that this is the very spirit through which the Apostle acknowledges that God <margin>1 Corinthians 12.</margin> works all things in all men. Sometimes He gives life by removing the northern cold, which causes death <note>original: "mortificans"</note>, and by dissolving the lethal or immobile freezing of the spirits—which had dazed them as if by a sleep-inducing and quiet spell of Morpheus <note>The Greek god of dreams</note>—using southern or eastern properties. This acts to liquefy, resolve, and give new motion and life to the spirits that were frozen and dazed by northern properties. And therefore David asserts in the aforementioned text: *He sends forth his word and melts them; as soon as he blows his wind, the waters flow.* From this we should observe that it is the word of God, or His incorruptible spirit, that animates the winds. Fourthly, that the same spirit which gives life and provides motion to the macrocosmic spirit—and at His pleasure kills, dazes, and ceases to act through life and motion by lethally freezing it—performs that very same duty when it is the Father's will <margin>Job 5.</margin> within the universal spirit of the microcosm or man, and indeed in every creature. Wherefore Job says: *It is God who wounds or strikes, and it is He who heals.* He shows the reason for this in the place cited before, as we can also find in <margin>Deuteronomy 32.</margin> Deuteronomy: *I will kill and I will make alive.* And Solomon: *You have the power of life and death in <margin>Ecclesiastes 7:14.</margin> your hand; you lead down to the grave and bring back again.* And Sirach: *Life and death, good and evil are from God.* Therefore, just as this secret and mystical spirit breathed the spirit of life into the dead, so that very spirit or breath is essential to the nature, property, and will of the Breather, who was meant to give life through growth and animation, and not through a dazing spirit. And hence it was that the created spirits, which were previously frozen and killed, are now made lively and are enclosed in an external body and an airy and bloody vehicle, which by the power and efficacy...

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that the same Divine Spirit that controls the winds and weather in the universe also governs life and death within the human body. He uses biblical references to Ezekiel and Job to demonstrate that God’s breath can either freeze the spirit into death or liquefy and revive it into life.</summary>
<keywords>Macrocosm, Microcosm, Spirit of Life, Weapon-salve, Robert Fludd, William Foster, four winds, contraction and expansion, animation, theology of nature</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense against William Foster. Fludd is explaining how the Divine Spirit, acting as a "breath of life," moves through the human body’s internal channels (veins and arteries) just as it moves through the heavens. He uses the analogy of the Sun to explain the heart's function and begins to refute Foster’s "Aristotelian" limits on how far a spiritual influence can travel, specifically regarding the "Weapon Salve" (a controversial treatment where an ointment was applied to the weapon that caused a wound, rather than the wound itself).</meta>

Fol. 11

### EXPRESSION OF MEMBER I.
C 3
<meta>catchword: ...run</meta>

...by the efficacy of this life-giving breath, He moved his spirit within the channels, or the veins and arteries, which are animated by the power of that spirit of life. And this is the reason why God enjoined such strict precepts concerning the blood of creatures (as mentioned before)—namely, that it should not be eaten, as was cited in the previous place: because in it is the spirit of life or the soul of the creature, in which resides the spiritual power of the **four winds**. For in this action of life, it exercises the same property in the hearts of creatures, or the **microcosm** <note>The "small world," referring to the human being as a reflection of the universe.</note>, as it does in the celestial Sun of the **macrocosm** <note>The "great world," or the universe as a whole.</note>. For just as the Sun warms, acting by **rarefaction** <note>The process of making something less dense; here, the thinning and expansion caused by heat.</note> and exciting motion—and for that reason reviving and multiplying both in vegetation and generation, in grains, plants, and other living things of the earth, pouring down from above the rays and light of life into the lower creatures—so also this incorruptible spirit or breath of life, thus infused into man, is the spiritual **Sun** of the microcosm. This spirit constitutes the heart as its **Tabernacle** (which the body of the celestial Sun represents), from which it sends out rays through the arteries and veins and animates the entire spirit of the human frame. It renders the blood agile, fluid, and vivid, constantly moving and working for the nourishment and preservation of the limbs, with both natural and vital spirits, bringing about both the bodily and spiritual growth and multiplication of parts in any specific body.

Now, however, let me address here in "three words" (as they say), though somewhat parenthetically, Mr. Foster’s **Aristotelian determined sphere of activity**. This is a concept which the ancient philosophers and scholastics have so thoroughly willed and tangled up in their external fantasies, without any central regard for this true and essential expansive or contractive power of the life-giving and nourishing spirit. Indeed, I would like to know whether any worldly philosophical axiom can confine or determine this principal spirit of the **four winds**, which blows and breathes equally in the macrocosm as in the microcosm—whenever, wherever, as far, and at whatever interval it pleases. First, I will give an example of its action from any wind in the macrocosm, and I will demonstrate to you how it commands, carries, and dilates <note>expands</note> the spirit of a vegetable creature. We can in no way gather the virtual operation of a vegetable creature except by its scent; for example, **Rosemary** and **Sassafras**, etc., emit their spirit into the air at a proportional distance, more or less according to the vivacity of the active spirit within them. Yet, nevertheless, we see that if a blast of a more vehement wind comes upon the emitted spirit of the creature, it violently dilates it from its center (the plant itself) and creates a very wide sphere of activity.

This is greater or lesser according to the power and violence of the wind. The cause is readily apparent and has been found to be only too true by those who travel by sea near Spain: for when the wind is an **Easterly**, they can perceive the air filled with the sweet scent of **Rosemary** up to thirty leagues <note>A league is roughly 3 miles; 30 leagues is about 90 miles.</note> from the shore. In a similar way, in Guiana and Virginia, at the same or even greater distance, the fragrant scent of the **sassafras** root along with other sweet-smelling woods is perceived by sailors on the coasts, and this sometimes happens before they can discern any land at all. What then shall we say of that very spirit which acts in the microcosm or man, when its insensible spirit or emanation tends sympathetically toward a place of the same nature as itself? I am referring to the **Ointment** <note>The famous "Weapon Salve."</note>, in which that same indivisible nature—either in the blood clinging to the weapon, or insofar as it has already penetrated the weapon—is immersed without any external sign of blood. Shall we believe it impossible for this spirit, by its emanation, to carry with it through the air the spirit of the wounded person’s nourishing nature, secretly enclosed in a certain **volatile salt**, to act upon the Ointment for the purpose of reawakening the spirits slumbering within it? 

"Not at all," says Mr. Foster. For the fragrant spirits of sassafras wood and rosemary are recognized by their scent, whereas the breath of such an emanating spirit, with its volatile vehicle of growth, cannot be perceived. This is truly an excellent argument for an "external and sensible" philosopher who, like **St. Thomas** <note>A reference to "Doubting Thomas," who required physical proof.</note>, refuses to believe anything unless he can touch, smell, or taste it. But men of "keen nostrils" <note>A metaphor for those with intellectual or spiritual discernment.</note> can easily gather that there is nothing external and visible that was not first internal and invisible. Nor can the reverse be said—that what was internal is now external. For an infinite number of internal and invisible actions are performed by God in the "chamber of nature," which do not fall within the sphere or capacity of the sensual or natural man, but are only to be believed by faith. For this reason, the Apostle says: 
<margin>Hebrews 11.</margin> *Through faith we understand that the world was framed by the word of God, so that things which are seen were not made of things which do appear.*
By which it is evidently proved that all things were invisible before they could be discerned by the senses. Consequently, it is the property of the external and carnal man to believe nothing except what he perceives by sense, and to say that if any thing appears to the senses which was not previously known, it must be diabolical and not from God. Yet the aforementioned Text, tracing back from invisible actions, attributes everything—not to the Devil, but to God—through the Word which is invisible to the senses. Therefore, all philosophers have agreed on this: that there is one spirit of life, which alone works in the human body. But this spirit, according to the diversity of its distinct duties...

<summary>Fludd argues that the human heart acts as a "Sun" for the body, distributing a divine life-breath through the blood. He refutes William Foster's skepticism by using the example of scents traveling long distances over the ocean to prove that invisible "spirits" can operate far beyond the physical body, supporting his theory of the Weapon Salve.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical defense of the "Weapon Salve." Fludd is arguing that the human soul and the "spirit of life" are not bound by physical distance, using both Neoplatonic philosophy and Biblical scripture to refute William Foster’s claim that such healing is impossible or demonic. This page picks up from the previous discussion on the spirit within the blood.</meta>

...receives a different name, and for that reason is called by some, in one respect, **rational**, in another, **desiring** <note>original: "concupiscibilis"; the faculty of the soul that seeks after what is pleasurable.</note>, and in a third, **spirited** <note>original: "irascibilis"; the faculty of the soul related to anger, courage, and defense.</note>. It is fit to be illuminated and to understand those things which are above it, below it, and within and with itself. For by this property or faculty, it knows God above itself; the Angels, who are impregnated by that very spirit; and whatever is encompassed in the whole circle of the heavens within itself. Such is the spiritual act of its central emanation, by reason of that mighty virtue granted to it by God. By the second and third faculties, it is inclined either to long for and affect something, or to avoid and flee from it—that is, either to love or to hate. And by this property, it exercises itself in **Sympathy** and **Antipathy** toward those things which are either agreeable or discordant to its specific nature. Therefore, in this duty, it operates powerfully in and around the effects of this *Weapon Salve* <note>original: "Unguenti armarij"; a medicinal ointment applied to the weapon that caused a wound rather than the wound itself, based on the theory of sympathetic resonance.</note>, since every affection proceeds from these latter two operations: For just as all joy and hope proceed from desire, because nature by its own nature rejoices, etc.; and therefore, by the union of a vivid emanation, the dead or "frozen" spirit in the *Salve* is nourished and brought to life. Conversely, the living soul or natural spirit (in which the supernatural spirit acts) is, by its own nature, liable to a species of spiritual pain and fear by reason of the weapons that inflicted violence upon it. To this belong the passions, which are perfected by the spirited <note>original: "irascibili"</note> part, insofar as it either grieves or is made sorrowful from the violence inflicted, or fears that it may grieve or become sorrowful thereafter. These four affections of the human spirit are the initiators and, as it were, the common subject of all virtuous and vicious actions that happen to man.

Now, to express the wide extension of that central spirit which radically operates in the vital spirits: the wiser Philosophers assert that it beholds itself within itself to the end that it may rightly understand itself. And when it wishes to know God, it raises itself above itself through its mental rays; it penetrates all things, beholds all things, both present and absent. When it pleases, it dwells in places across the sea and searches out our hidden things; indeed, in a moment, it emits and directs rays to the most remote borders of the whole world and scrutinizes its secrets. It descends into the deep, and from there ascends again into heaven and adheres firmly to *Christ*, and is made one with *Christ*. Is it then necessary that the infinite power of this all-penetrating spirit, according to *Master Foster’s* Dogma, be determined and limited by any imaginary sphere of activity assigned by the vain Philosophy of the Heathens—which, as the *Apostle* says, is fashioned from the traditions of men and the world, and not of God? Does he not warn us to take care lest we be deceived by such philosophical doctrine, which disagrees with the rules of *Christ*, in whom the fullness of the Divinity dwells bodily? And must we, to obey the fantastical idea of *Master Foster*, violate the laws of the *Apostle* so as to be deluded by his spurious philosophy? But let us return to our purpose.

All this mentioned above being rightly considered—namely, the universal <note>original: "Catholicâ"</note> nature of the spirit which inspires life into creatures, the indivisibility and individuality of the Giver and the gift that is given (for the divine essence is indivisible, and therefore the divine spirit imparted to the creature is continuous and undivided from Him who gives it)—as well as the infinity of its extension, insofar as it is determined by no limits (and for this reason it is said: "the spirit blows where it pleases" <note>John 3:8</note>, and that without obstacle). This spirit can turn itself from a vivid and active power into a potential, "freezing," and lethal property in the creature by withdrawing its actual rays from the circumference of the creature (where it sent them for the sake of lively actions) back into the center—that is, into itself—where (in respect to the creature) it rests. Thus, it deprives the spirit of the creature of motion, act, and life through coagulation, which it formerly imparted by its indignant activity to the natural spirits to generate blood, fat, flesh, and bones. For this reason, I say—namely, because of the presence of this incorruptible spirit in the blood of the creature—God strictly forbade the Israelites from eating blood: <margin>*Leviticus 17.*</margin> because (as the *Text* says) the life of the flesh is in the blood. Observe here, therefore, that the spirit of life is from God, who gives life to all things: the life of the blood and the fat is in this spirit and from this spirit. <margin>*Leviticus 3. Acts 17:24.*</margin> Wherefore it stands written elsewhere: "You shall not eat the blood and fat at all: for the life of the flesh is in the blood, and the life of the bones is in the flesh." It is easy, therefore, to discern what a concatenation <note>A chain-like connection.</note> of members occurs here in succession, which derive their life from one and the same radical essence or spirit, and are thereby compelled to sympathize with one and the same harmony in the composition of the creature. Since He created the whole human race from "one blood," as *St. Paul* says quite fittingly, and consequently, since all flesh and bones are produced from one blood, it is necessary that there be a great relation between them and human blood in general—and consequently between the blood and the salve that is made from them.

These things being rightly considered and weighed as infallible foundations, we conclude in this manner: In the proposed question, these five objects are to be observed by us: first, the wound; second, the blood flowing from the wound; third, the means of conduction and transfer from the salve to the wound across that distance; fourth, the nature of the salve; and finally, the mode of operation by which the cure is perfected.

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that the human spirit is an extension of God's indivisible spirit, capable of instantaneous connection across distances through sympathy. He uses this theological and philosophical framework to justify how a "Weapon Salve" can heal a wound by interacting with the spirit remaining in the blood.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical defense of the "Weapon Salve." Fludd is arguing that the human soul and the "spirit of life" are not bound by physical distance, using both Neoplatonic philosophy and Biblical scripture to refute William Foster’s claim that such healing is impossible or demonic. This page focuses on the nature of a wound and the behavior of the life-force within spilled blood.</meta>

# EXPLANATION OF THE FIRST PART

First, therefore, as it pertains to the wound, it is a violation of the work which the **spirit of life** <note>original: "spiritus vitæ"</note> was performing—namely, the shedding of blood, in which the spirit of life is carried and moves; it is an impediment and diversion of the natural course of the humors, a division and breaking of the fat, flesh, and other similar parts from their integrity and continuity; it is a stumbling block and an injury to that peaceful act of life, produced through God’s incorruptible spirit, which, by this property or attribute of its own, is fit for **giving life to all things** <note>original: "vivificandum omnia"</note>. For this reason, this radical, acting spirit is involved in this unnatural business or act, as it finds its work hindered and its essential action disturbed by the wound or the violence offered to it. For, since the blood is its vehicle, and its life-giving act was established to circulate in the organic blood and to produce from there a transformation into flesh and other parts for the sake of vegetation, multiplication, and the preservation of the individual; now that same blood is poured out from the mouth of the wound and made useless and of no effect. The body (toward whose animation this secret spirit is perpetually and diligently inclined) is weakened and consumed by exhaustion. Therefore, as an incorruptible and life-giving nature, it intended to rectify its human spirit through its vivid activity; thus, **by Hercules!** <note>original: "Herclé"; a common Latin exclamation of emphasis</note>, it is ready and prompt to resist all violence offered, and to correct and restore whatever the violent rupture caused. It is like the prudent spider which, when its web is finished and partially corrupted, applies all its diligence to lead it back to its original perfection.

Secondly, although the blood is evacuated from the wound, as it is the vehicle of the spirit of life, it nevertheless retains this spirit of life within itself, but in another property. For it no longer acts toward life—that is, it does not emit its rays from the center to the circumference to produce life—but on the contrary, as if it were burdened and grieving by the violence of the act, it contracts itself from the circumference to the center. This means it moves from action in the creature’s circumference back into itself, after it has contracted itself into its center, where it seems to rest. And so it leaves its airy vehicle, as if frozen, stupefied, and dead. And here the mystery is revealed—namely, the reason why, when a **murderer** <note>original: "homicida"</note> is brought before the slain person, the spirit resting centrally in the blood miraculously emanates and flows out, and makes the blood fluid again, as if incited and moved by a similar spirit acting and activating antipathetically from the murderer’s blood. For, as I said before, this spirit in its **wrathful** <note>original: "irascibili"</note> property is just as fit for pursuing with hatred as it is in its **desiring** <note>original: "concupiscibili"</note> property for loving. <margin>Genesis 9</margin> And for this reason, the Text teaches us that the blood of the slain is required not only from man but also from the beast, if it was shed by it. And again, the blood of that creature is not to be eaten; which would be a superficial command only, unless the spirit of life rested in the blood after its shedding; <margin>Leviticus 17</margin> just as this reason (that the blood of animals caught in hunting or fowling ought to be buried in the earth) would prove to have little <margin>Numbers 35</margin> validity or efficacy. For this reason, it is said elsewhere: the earth defiled by blood cannot be expiated except by the blood of him who shed another's blood. Finally, why would it be said that the blood is the seat of the spirit of life, unless the spirit shared in it after it was poured from the wound, frozen and as if dead, and resting in its center? Indeed, this spirit altogether leaves and dismisses the blood of the dead, since its life (as said) is in the blood; yet it does not leave the **bones** <note>original: "ossa"</note> themselves, insofar as they partake of the nature of the most earthy part of the flesh. Hence it happened that when certain thieves had thrown the corpse of someone they had killed into the tomb of **Elisha**, the slain man, by the mere contact with the bones of Elisha, rose again to life—which could hardly have been achieved unless his divine as well as life-giving nature had shared with his bones. And this is why it is said that after Elisha's death his body prophesied, and that in life he performed miracles, and in death his works were miraculous. Lastly, the learned and wise Philosophers (speaking enigmatically of this spirit) say that there is in the body a certain small bone called **Luz**, which is to remain with a man until the last day, and will cause him to rise again. But these things are to be understood according to their proper sense, not vulgarly. Let it be fixed in the memory, I pray, that the contact with Elisha’s bones caused the dead to rise from death to life.

In the third place, I come to the manner of conduction and translation of the blood from the wound to the **Ointment** <note>original: "Unguentum"</note>. The blood is taken from the living fountain of that wounded man's blood, either as it is smeared on the weapons that inflicted the wound, or as it is bound to a staff, or iron, or some other thing, and so it is transferred to the ointment at any moderate distance. Now, indeed, the reason must be shown how it is possible that any certain relation can exist between the wound and the ointment. For, as **Foster** <note>original: "Fosterus"; referring to William Foster, a critic of the weapon salve</note> says, there can be castles, mountains, walls, and thick air between the Ointment and the wounded person, which might break or entirely impede this cure.

Furthermore, we must hold it sharply fixed in memory that we have clearly explained in our preceding discourse the excellence of the animating spirit, in which is every virtue and every property of the four winds, and which becomes the spirit of spirits; and the spirit breathes where it wills; what, I ask, can hinder its act or operation? And at what distance or interval can its activity...

C 4
<meta>catchword: its (ejus)</meta>

<summary>Fludd argues that blood contains a "spirit of life" that remains active even after leaving the body, citing Biblical examples and the phenomenon of "cruentation" (corpses bleeding in the presence of their killers) to justify how a distant ointment can affect a wound. He defends the sympathetic connection between blood on a weapon and the wounded person, dismissing physical obstacles like walls or mountains.</summary>
