<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense of "sympathetic magic" against the attacks of the parson William Foster. Fludd is arguing that spirits (both good and evil) can interact with physical matter like air and blood, just as a scent or a disease spreads through the atmosphere.</meta>

# FOSTER’S SPONGES.
<meta>catchword: which</meta>

...his own spirit, which, by uniting or mixing itself with the air, infects it with its own fragrant nature. In this way, an actual communication is established between the air and the smoke; between the spirit of one thing and another; between a secret place—such as a latrine—or a pestilential, blistered contagion and the infected spirit of the air. This air then communicates that infection to the spirit of the person smelling it. Again, do we not see in a person infected by an epidemic that the spirit was corrupted first? By its virtual contact, it infected not only the blood, but the divisible spirits within the blood. And do we not also see that same invisible spirit, infecting in this way, also invisibly infects the invisible air all around? Although it has partially exhaled from the body and blood, it nonetheless maintains such a communication with the blood that the invisible smoke infects others outside, while still continuing its pursuit of the person at home. Therefore, if a pestilential *Althoin* <note>original: "Althoin pestilentiale." A term used by Fludd to describe a poisonous or infectious spiritual vapor.</note> arising in one man (which the Prophet Habakkuk calls the Demon, or the Noonday Devil) <note>original: "Dæmonem Meridianolem." A reference to Psalm 91:6, though Fludd attributes the concept here to Habakkuk; it refers to a spirit of destruction that strikes in plain sight.</note> infects the spiritual blood of another, it is not the physical carbuncle <term>carbuncle: A severe, painful skin infection or boil, often associated with the plague.</term> that does it, but the spirit infected within it. We thus attribute the physical carbuncle to the "body" assumed by the Devil. Therefore, as we see, once the carbuncle of one person has touched another, the spirit—by reason of their mutual similarity—easily communicates with the other. So much so that, even if the one infected by the carbuncle moves away to any distance, the malignant spirit nonetheless works together with the recently infected blood and transforms it entirely into its own malignant nature.

<margin>John 13.</margin>
Is it not possible, then, that the spiritual malignancy of the Devil, through some contact-based suction of blood, might contaminate and disturb the spirit of the drawn human blood with its own spirit's malignancy? And might it not leave it so infected and changed into its own nature, just as the poisonous spirit of pestilence creates an epidemic disposition? Did not the Devil work the same effect with the spirit of Judas, when it was said: *The Devil put it into the heart of Judas to betray Christ*? That is, he infected his spirit first, and those spirits corrupted his thoughts or mind. For without some mediating support, even the Devil, though cast down from heaven, cannot stir the "heaven" within the human intellect.

But to answer Foster, who shines in his own armor: I understand that most sagacious man, who can smell a mouse so quickly. What does he truly think—that Devils do not possess thin bodies? Indeed they do. For he has for this (as he says) the authorities of Scripture, the Councils, the Church Fathers, and the Scholastics, which only need to be confirmed. But along the way, and as if in passing, the Doctor says that I, who impiously attribute "composition" <term>composition: The state of being made of parts. Foster accused Fludd of saying God is "composed" of parts like a physical object.</term> to God, falsely dare to attribute "corporeality" to Devils. In the first place, Foster holds the Devil, the Father of Lies, as his foster father, who knows how to nourish and cherish a lie upon anything. But as I said before, that monk with the snotty nose, Mersenne <note>original: "mucosae naris monachus Mersennus." Fludd is using a stinging 17th-century insult against the French philosopher Marin Mersenne, suggesting he is foolish or immature.</note>, says this because I asserted that the Spirit of the Lord filled and animated the celestial spirit. Therefore (says Mersenne), Fludd makes God a part of a composition. And so, our Master Presbyter, regarding the words of that monk as an oracle without any weighing of the meaning of this business, blabs the monk’s own words like a parrot: "The Doctor impiously attributes composition to God." But if I say God is *in* a composition, I do not mean Him as a component part, but as the sole Composer within the composition. Again, if He were absent from the composition, the Word would not be said to be *made flesh*; nor would the incorruptible Spirit be in all things; nor could God be said by the Apostle to be *above all and in all.*

But let us set aside this parrot-like or parasitic chatter and heap of words. He brings forth testimonies of Scripture—and what are they? St. Paul has this: *We wrestle not against flesh and blood, but against spiritual wickedness* (or evil spirits) *in high places.* And for this reason, Christ said: *Handle me and touch me; for a spirit has not flesh and bones, as you see me have.*

->Foster.<-

->But Devils cannot be handled<-
->nor felt.<-

->**Therefore, they do not have bodies.**<-

->Fludd.<-

Behold here a proud argument: because Devils, as they exist in their thin, airy bodies, cannot be handled, therefore they have no bodily substance at all! Truly, I would like to know from this sharp arguer: what organic body does this spirit have for speech when he tempted Christ with reciprocal words and voices? Does he think the air itself (which is the Devil's external "body," as will soon be proved) is not a spiritual body when it can be felt, heard, or understood, even if it is not like flesh and bones? Does not the Apostle mention a certain spiritual or celestial and thin body, and a terrestrial and gross body?

It is very true that there are some of the Fathers and Scholastics who hold the opinion that Angels are absolutely incorporeal, such as John of Damascus, Thomas Aquinas, Dionysius, etc. But there are just as many on the other side—indeed, even more learned ones—who are of the contrary opinion: that...

He also debates the physical nature of demons, arguing they possess "airy" spiritual bodies despite being intangible to human touch.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical defense against William Foster. Having previously discussed how infections and scents move through the air, Fludd now transitions into a deep theological and "natural-philosophical" argument regarding the physical nature of angels and demons. He argues that they are not purely abstract spirits but possess "aerial bodies" that allow them to interact with the material world.</meta>

# EXPRESSION OF PART III. Folio 28.
-> G 4 <-
<meta>catchword: as-</meta>

# EXPRESSION OF PART III.

...concerning the bodily existence of these spirits; they say simply that an Angel is a corporeal substance, and consequently that Angels can, without any error, be called corporeal creatures. Among the number of the Fathers who hold this view are Basil, Origen, Gregory, and Augustine, as well as Isidore and Peter Lombard. Among the philosophers, Hermes Trismegistus <note>original: "Mercurius Trismegistus." The legendary Hellenistic figure often cited as the founder of alchemy and Hermetic philosophy.</note> and the entire school of the Academics agree. As for St. Augustine, he says in his book *On Genesis* in express words that "demons are aerial and fiery animals," and consequently he assigns to them aerial bodies. Elsewhere, he affirms that Angels, at their creation, possessed aerial bodies—namely, bodies formed and shaped from the purer part of the upper air, which is rendered more ready and proper for acting than for suffering <note>In Aristotelian philosophy, "acting" is the active force, while "suffering" or "undergoing" refers to being acted upon by an external force.</note>. He further asserts that the malignant Angels, by reason of their fall, were changed in regard to their bodies and their outward existence into the nature of "grosser air," so that they might be made more ready to suffer the torments of fire. And Peter Lombard says: "Angels take the bodies in which they appear to men from the highest air, and they put on a solid appearance from the celestial element, so that they may be clearly demonstrated to human sight." Thus, it is evident that Augustine and Lombard agree on this point. Similarly, Basil teaches us what kind of bodies Angels have when he says: "They are thin, aerial, and pure spirits." Again, that wise man Artephius <note>A famous medieval alchemist, often claimed to have lived for a thousand years.</note> says that "the exterior of the devil is air, but his interior is fire." For this reason, he demonstrates that it is easy for the devil—specifically regarding his exterior or body—to insinuate himself into and participate with the aerial and sanguine spirit in man, and consequently to produce in him hot and fiery diseases.

But why should we rest merely (as is the custom of later writers) on bare authorities? I shall now move to plain philosophical proofs, by which I will clearly explain and demonstrate that Angels have souls and bodies, or internal and external parts. First, it must be known that if they were "Identity"—that is, consisting of a single, simple formal existence—they would be one and the same in essence with God their Creator, who is called absolute and simple Identity or Unity. But in truth, insofar as they are composed of two things—namely, of light, which is the ray of God that they receive to be formed and become creatures; and of spirit, which, like a polished mirror, receives the glory of that heavenly light—they are called "Alterity" <note>original: "Alteritas." A philosophical term meaning "otherness" or being composed of distinct parts, as opposed to God's "Oneness."</note>, meaning they are composed of two. This is expressed in very vivid colors by St. Dionysius <note>original: "D. Denim," likely a typo for "Dionysium," referring to Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite, an influential mystical theologian.</note> when he calls them *Algamatha* <note>original: "Algamatha." A term derived from Hebrew or Syriac meaning "polished mirrors" or "statues," used by Dionysius to describe how angels reflect divine light.</note>, that is, highly polished mirrors receiving the light of God. He therefore defines an Angel as the image of God, an emanation of hidden light, and a most pure and splendid mirror. And John Damascene says they are intellectual spirits, having light (as their soul) from the Prime Light. And Solomon, depicting the prince of all—

***

<margin>Wisdom 7.</margin>
<margin>2 Peter 3.</margin>
<margin>Genesis 1.</margin>
—Angels (who, as the book of Ecclesiasticus says, was created before all things) says he is the "Brightness of eternal light and the spotless mirror of the Majesty of God." From this it is clear that the internal part and, as it were, the soul of an Angel is the splendor of divine emanation; his internal, polished, and pure aerial part is his spiritual body, which receives this light. For we must note that in the beginning, the heaven and the earth were made "from the waters and through the water" consisting by the Word, as St. Peter holds. Therefore, the whole world was composed of an internal or invisible part, which is the soul or spirit animated by the Word of God, and an external and visible part, earth and water, which is the body. Thus, it necessarily follows that every creature is composed of an internal or active soul, and some external or organic receptacle for that soul, which is the body. Is it not manifest that when the Spirit of God <note>original: "Ruach-Elohim." Hebrew for the Spirit or Breath of God.</note> moved upon the waters, the water was the universal "patient" <note>The thing being acted upon.</note> and the spirit was the internal "agent"? For St. Augustine says on Genesis: "The Spirit was borne over the waters, imparting to them a fiery vigor." Thus, the created spiritual waters were animated by the spiritual breath in the created universe and by the light of life; the spirit of this light in any creature is the spirit of life, and for that reason, their central soul; and the animated creature is the body. Therefore, just as the purer and more spiritual part of the water or air is the exterior of the Angel, so his interior is the luminous act of the spirit of God.

<margin>2 Samuel 22.</margin>
<margin>Job 22:13.</margin>
<margin>Psalm 104.</margin>
<margin>Numbers 11:25.</margin>
<margin>Psalm 105:47.</margin>
Now, therefore, I conclude thus: if the external substance of an Angel is air (for it must be either from spiritual water or from the substance of God, which is purely formal and not material), we know by the rules of philosophy that thinned air is fire, and conversely, thickened air is vapor, mist, or cloud. Thus, through thickening, invisible air becomes a visible substance; indeed, it even becomes a corporeal vocal organ, as appears in lightning—for what is the soul of a cloud unless it has its own cloudy organ or corporeal instrument? It would not speak in the thunder <note>original: "Tonitru."</note>. We find, therefore, from the Holy Scriptures, that God is said to speak from His "organic cloud," and for that very reason the Text calls it in one place the "hiding place of God," in another the "Tabernacle of God," and in another the "vehicle and chariot of Jehovah." Thus, if Jehovah makes this organic Tabernacle to give His voice to mortal ears (as the Scriptures testify in many places), it is no sin to say that these lower spirits have aerial substances for their external bodies. This being granted, what would prevent spirits—by the contraction of their external substances—from appearing visibly whenever they please, and speaking with man "organically," just as that tempting spirit spoke to Christ? And again, by a sudden dilation of that same external aerial spirit, they might become invisible; no differently than smoke vanishes by dilation, or a cloud or mist composed of compacted and thick air...

<summary>Robert Fludd argues that angels and demons possess "aerial bodies" composed of light and spiritualized air, allowing them to interact with the physical world. He supports this by citing Church Fathers and Neoplatonic philosophy, suggesting that all created beings require an external "receptacle" for their internal divine soul.</summary>

<keywords>Angels, aerial bodies, Hermes Trismegistus, Peter Lombard, Dionysius the Areopagite, Algamatha, Spirit of God, Ruach-Elohim, thunder, Jehovah, Robert Fludd</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical defense of the "weapon-salve" against the attacks of the parson William Foster. Fludd is currently explaining the "physics of spirits"—how spiritual beings like angels and even the resurrected Christ can interact with the physical world by changing the density of their "aerial bodies."</meta>

### FOSTER’S SPONGE.
<meta>catchword: Now</meta>

...so often passes invisibly without the appearance of a single drop of rain? Was it not a strange thing that Christ himself, who possessed flesh and bones, appeared even when the doors were closed and vanished immediately? And yet, if we consider that after he had risen, he had put on a spiritual body, for that very reason he was able to set aside his visible and tangible Tabernacle <note>A biblical term for the physical body as a temporary dwelling for the soul.</note> and, through thinning and expansion, become as subtle and impalpable as he pleased, as one who possesses a spiritual body. And thus, he can appear and vanish in an instant. It is a wonderful speculation to consider and weigh in the proper way how God works in this world through **contraction** and **dilatation** <note>These are Fludd’s primary physical principles: "contraction" (shrinking/thickening/cold) and "dilatation" (expanding/thinning/heat).</note>, through deprivation and opposition, through darkness and light, and through appearing and disappearing.

Just as we see when his spirit is moved from the north, the common air—by the contractive nature of that spirit—is turned from invisibility into visibility, from transparency into opacity, from air into snow, hail, frost, and ice; from lightness into weightiness, and from agility and mobility into fixity and immobility. On the contrary, by his breath from the east or the South, these same bodies are changed back into waters, and water into air, and air into fire by expansion. Finally, earthly corporeality is changed into aerial and celestial corporeality; hardness into saltiness <note>Likely referring to the "saline" state in alchemy, a middle ground between solid and liquid.</note>, thickness into subtlety, opacity into transparency, fixity into mobility, rest into action, and darkness into light! And lastly, the contraction effected by this divine spirit is turned into expansion, and visibility into invisibility.

What more is there to say? If every kind of Angel has its outward form from the aerial spirit of the world, and its internal action from this external life-giving spirit in which is the property of the four winds (and <margin>Ezekiel 37:9</margin> <note>The original margin notes Chapter 36, but the "four winds" prophecy is found in Ezekiel 37:9.</note> therefore, says the Prophet: *Come, O spirit, from the four winds*, by which he proves that this one spirit, as the essential actor in the four winds, possesses the properties of the four winds within itself, by which it performs all things; wherefore the Prophet calls it forth from the four winds), we ought in no way to doubt that by the power of that internal act and the substance of their external air, they can contract themselves from spiritual, fiery, and aerial invisibility into a cloudy or watery, and even an earthly visibility, or a snowy or icy nature.

This is especially true of the gross, malignant, and dark spirits who, through their fall, have put on a "grosser air" (as Augustine says). And this is the reason why Satan is called by the Apostle the "prince of the air." Hence it is that the Devil or malignant spirits, in their contraction, turn themselves into the solid or firm figures of men or beasts; and to the touch, they appear so intensely cold (according to Foster’s own confession) <note>Fludd is likely referencing a specific anecdote or claim in Foster's book *Hoplocrisma-Spongus* regarding the coldness of demonic apparitions.</note>. This is because the spirit by which they live, in contracting itself from the circumference (or expanded air) toward the center (or contracted earth), leaves the external or aerial composition compact and cold like ice. For it is by its emanation or expansion from the center to the circumference that it kindles natural heat in the exterior of any creature.

To conclude against those who deny that spirits have corporeality: it is most certain that where there is the "rare" and the "dense" <note>In Aristotelian physics, "rare" means thin/expanded, and "dense" means thick/contracted.</note>, there is, consequently, corporeality, whether it be thin or thick. For whatever in its substance can be transmuted into a thinner or thicker body must be corporeal, even if it is not a visible body. Thus, a star in the heavens is called a denser part of its orb. Thus also fire can be condensed into air, and air into water, and water into earth; and again, earth can be thinned into water, and water into air, and air into fire. For such is the natural rotation of the Elements.

Now, the external part of the Angels must be created from the spiritual substance of the higher world, or not at all (according to the tenor of Basil). Consequently, it is corporeal, though of a thinner or thicker consistency according to the dignity of the <margin>Psalm 104:4</margin> Angel. Does not David acknowledge as much in these words:
> Who makes his Angels spirits (or windy air) and his ministers a burning fire.

And therefore, it is a disgrace for mysteries such as these—which are quite manifest to one who considers them—to be mocked by the ignorant or unskillful; and for the works and operations of the spirit of God in the common Element of the world to be judged as the works of the Devil. Thus, in their blindness, they take evil for good and darkness for light. The Prophet points out this kind of person in these words:
<margin>Isaiah 5:20</margin>
> Woe to those who speak good of evil, and evil of good; who put darkness for light, and light for darkness; who put bitter for sweet, and sweet for bitter! Woe to those who are wise in their own eyes, and prudent in their own sight.

And so, judicious reader, one may perceive from what has been explained in this section how powerless my adversary’s "sponge" has been to wipe away and delete even the smallest jot of that natural value and divine virtue which I ascribed to the Weapon-Salve <note>original: "Vnguento armario." A sympathetic ointment believed to heal wounds by being applied to the weapon that caused them.</note> in my *Mystical Anatomy*. Therefore, as far as I can see, he must find more substantial means than this "sponge" (which is a clear proof of a weakened brain and a fantastical wit) to subvert a medicine of such great importance and wonderful virtue in its operation. There must be, I say, the strongest ropes instead of this very light sponge to move the foundation of truth. And yet, I fear they will break before they can draw wise men to the belief that the beneficial gifts of healing in this weapon-salve proceed from the Devil, rather than from God and His kind clemency and mercy, who alone is the giver of health and goodness.

<summary>Fludd explains how spiritual beings use "contraction and dilatation" of the air to become visible or cold, arguing that the air is a medium for divine action. He concludes by dismissing Foster's "sponge" as a weak attempt to label a natural, God-given healing remedy (the weapon-salve) as demonic.</summary>
<keywords>contraction, dilatation, spiritual body, Christ, Ezekiel, elements, rotation of elements, angels, Satan, air, William Foster, weapon-salve, unguentum armarium, Anatomia mystica, Psalm 104, Isaiah 5:20, Robert Fludd</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s vigorous defense of his "weapon-salve" against the criticisms of William Foster, a parson who had published a pamphlet titled "The Sponge" to "wipe away" Fludd's theories. Fludd is currently pointing out contradictions in Foster's argument—specifically how Foster labels the cure "diabolic" while simultaneously attributing its success to simple hygiene.</meta>

# EXPOSITION OF PART III.

Now indeed I must recall to your memory, as if in passing, the absurdity of our sponge-bearing author <note>A mocking reference to William Foster, who wrote *The Sponge to Wipe Away the Weapon-Salve*.</note>: For he says that this method of curing is Diabolical, yet afterward he seems to attribute its effects to human urine. These are his very words:

<margin>Page 8</margin>
Dr. Fludd’s directions are that the weapon should be left in the medicine chest while the patient recovers, and that the wound be kept clean with a small linen cloth, moistened every morning in his own urine. Whether this be a fallacy or not, I leave to the judgment of those skilled in surgery; for the Doctor may be certain and persuaded that if a wound is healed by being kept clean, they will tell him it would have formed a scar without the weapon-salve.

->Fludd<-

To this I respond: a liar ought to have a good memory. For our Foster should remember that he said elsewhere: *The Devil secretly applies some other operative medicine to cure the wound and to deceive the incredulous wandering physicians, etc.* Yet here he reveals that the cause of the cure is the cleaning of the wound with a cloth soaked in the urine of the wounded man, applied not by the Devil, but by a human being—saying that the cure consists in that alone without the **weapon-salve**. He calls all experienced surgical artists as witnesses to confirm his words are true. Since this is the case, what blessing of God comes from the cunning of the Devil in this cure? Or in what way can it appear Diabolical? Or how does the Devil apply anything in a "cunning way" to deceive "incredulous wandering physicians"? O, the staggering of a reeling brain that forgets its own argument and backwards devours its own words!

***

### CHAPTER VII.

->*In which the whole subject is contracted*<-
->*and abbreviated into a few words.*<-

It is possible to perceive, candid reader, from these things which have now been said, that although our adversary has attempted—as the Jews did to our Savior—to crucify or rather to commit to oblivion the truth, both of the operative power of the **weapon-salve** and of my own honest efforts, upon a "caco-magical" <note>original: *cacomagica*; meaning "evil-magical" or related to black magic.</note> cross of insults; and although in our agony (namely, when our reputation lies bleeding), he offers us a destructive or abolishing sponge swollen with vinegar and gall—by which I mean the sharp seasoning of calumny and the bitter flavor of his inhuman indignation, instead of Christian consolation; indeed, I say, although the stormy blast of his most bitter spirit has done what it could to bring shipwreck to the reputation of both, and like some Jonah, to cast it into the troubled seas of this world’s judgment, where the swelling waves of various passions clash impetuously and resist one another (for from that comes the theorem: *as many men, so many opinions*); how is it possible to please and humor everyone in their own whims? 

And although Foster’s "spongy" Leviathan <note>A biblical sea monster, here representing Foster’s prideful attack.</note>—that is, his proudly swelling and insulting whale—might absorb for a time into its belly of oblivion the honor and estimation of these salves and myself for a short interval; nevertheless (I give thanks to my God), He has granted me that grace to so squeeze and wring that deceptive whale of his, or that devouring sponge (which is also a progeny of the sea), that even now it is compelled in the same manner to vomit that truth back onto our shore—a shore not a little infected with disbelief in this method of healing. Just as the true whale once vomited the Prophet Jonah onto the shore of the incredulous Ninevites, so may the disbelief and suspicion of such zealous men, who were seduced by our adversary’s Leviathan or spongy monster, be more easily abolished. This is done by making the truth, once absorbed and devoured, live again and, as it were, shine forth and rise up, so that by its light all such dark clouds which gave birth to and nourished such incredulity—namely, the religious veil of Mr. Foster’s worldly cunning, the error of his doctrine, and the healing power which he so falsely ascribes to the Devil—may be uncovered to the eyes of any wise and judicious reader. Thus, like the Ninevites, the incredulous may repent of their error and be converted from such idolatrous enticements, which persuaded them to ascribe the pious and beneficial gifts of healing through the **weapon-salve** falsely to the prince of darkness. He, through the error of man, usurps for himself the title of prince or ruler of this world, which by right, and that from all eternity, belongs to the true God of light, who made both heaven and earth and enriched them with every kind of virtue. 

But, if in the future it happens that certain obstinate unbelievers (I mean those apparently jealous without understanding, of whom St. Paul speaks) return like dogs to their vomit; I must therefore say and confess that they only act according to their kind, and for that reason they are to be pitied rather than envied or hated. Consequently, the words of our Savior, so sinisterly applied by our adversary in his dedicatory epistle, can very fittingly be poured out to God for these men: *Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do.* For this cause therefore...

<sig>H</sig>
<meta>catchword: ...tur (the first syllable of the next page's word, likely "consequentur")</meta>

He uses the biblical allegory of Jonah and the Whale to describe how he is rescuing the "truth" of his medicine from Foster's "spongy" attacks.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s vigorous defense of his "weapon-salve" against the criticisms of William Foster. Fludd is arguing that the power to heal belongs exclusively to God and His "Word," rather than being a diabolical trick, as Foster claims.</meta>

# THE FOSTERIAN SPONGE

I especially direct this pamphlet of mine to all who are zealous, intelligent, and judiciously learned, humbly asking and beseeching them to receive this brief sketch and model of our dispute into their more mature considerations, and to weigh every part of it truly (with all partiality and schism set aside) in the scale and balance of their honest and pious discretion.

Let them examine, I say, in their most choice thoughts—and that seriously—whether this cure proceeds from that virtuous gift which God imparted to these His natural creatures at creation and continued in them through the succession of generation and multiplication by His word (complete in all its parts) unto this very day; or from the mere act and assistance of the devil. The devil's nature, immediately after his creation through his fall, became different: to strike, to afflict, and to destroy, but truly not to heal or to show himself as such a benefactor to the human race. Let them consider the words of the Apostle, affirming that God works all in all; and therefore He acts whenever He chooses in and through the Devil only toward disease, death, and destruction, but not toward health and preservation. Observe the text of David asserting that God healed those who had been struck by diseases with His word when they returned to Him; and does He not heal all things through the spoken word? Entirely so: for Solomon says: He extended His mercy to the one afflicted by serpents, and sends forth His word to heal them—the very same word of His which heals all things. But the Devil is neither God, nor the word of God; wherefore I shall conclude with this adamantine <note>unbreakable or rock-solid</note> argument.

<margin>Psalm 107:20</margin>
<margin>Wisdom 16:10</margin>
If our merciful God heals all things by His kindness, and that through His word, the Devil can heal nothing, and consequently he cannot deceitfully simulate a cure under the shadow and pretext of the **weapon-salve** <note>original: "unguenti armarij"</note>.

The major premise is evident, because if the word heals all things, that superior generality includes all inferior particulars, and therefore all absolute faculty of healing is taken away from the Devil.

<margin>Psalm 107:20-21</margin>
<margin>Wisdom 16:10</margin>
The minor premise is similarly confirmed by the words spoken before: And first by the Prophet David: *He sends forth His word and heals them, and delivers them from their graves; let them confess before the Lord His friendly kindness, etc.* From which it appears that it was His pleasant kindness, not His severity and vengeance, which healed and cured through His word. And again from Solomon: *But the poison of the venomous Dragon could not overcome Your Sons: for Your mercy came to help them and thoroughly healed them: For neither herb nor <term>malagma</term> <note>A soft, medicinal plaster or poultice applied to the skin.</note> cured them, but Your word, O Lord, which heals all things: for You have the power of life and death and lead down to the gates of hell and lead back again, etc.*

Now indeed I would like to know whether it befits any true Christian to hold the opinion that the Devil can command God’s mercy, and thus be the master of His word at his own pleasure for the purpose of healing God's creatures—especially someone created according to His own image—for the sake of some sorcerous stratagem, namely to win both body and soul away from God? Does not Job say: *In the hand of the Lord is the life of every living creature and the spirit of all flesh?* <note>Job 12:10</note> Finally, as Saint John truly asserts, life was in the Word; so it is certain that every healing and restoring virtue is from this life-giving power in the Word, not truly from the privative <note>lacking or taking away</note> power of the Devil, in which, by contrast, there is death and destruction. Furthermore, I would have you note these words of the Apostle: *Now there are diverse gifts, but the same Spirit; and there are diversities of operations, but it is the same God who works all in all. But to one is given through the Spirit the gift of wisdom, to another the word of knowledge, to another Faith, and to another the gift of healing through the same spirit, etc.*
<margin>Job 12:11</margin>
<margin>1 Corinthians 12</margin>

Can any honest Christian imagine that this one Spirit, who alone works these things, is the Devil? Not at all: for in the third verse the Apostle calls Him the Holy Spirit. What then? Does the Devil heal through the Holy Spirit? Or would the Holy Spirit grant His beneficial gift of healing to the evil spirit, to deceive the human race and to strip God of His right? Far be it! Instead, with justice, attribute to God what is God's, assigning to the Devil that property which he received from God the Creator in the beginning: the former Spirit was ordained from all creation in his office as good, life-giving, and quickening; but the latter as evil, destroying, and mortifying: for it is said in the Prophet in the person of God: *I have created the destroyer to scatter.*
<margin>Isaiah 54:16</margin>

Boldly, therefore, I shall conclude and finish this pamphlet of mine, or modest discourse, just as I began; namely with this religious verse cited in the divine hymn of the royal Psalmist in honor of God, and to take away the power from either the Devil or any other creature of working essential miracles by themselves or through themselves.

<margin>Psalm 71:18</margin>
> Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, who alone does wondrous things.

Or as it says elsewhere:

<margin>Psalm 135:3</margin>
> Give thanks to the Lord of Lords, for His mercy endures forever, who alone does great wonders.

And therefore, if He is the Lord of Lords only, or alone, He does not have any mortal man to help Him; if He is alone, then not any Angel in heaven; and finally, if it be God alone and only, then not any infernal Devil, nor Demon, nor spirit of the Element of fire or air, or...

<summary>Fludd argues that healing is a divine power belonging solely to God's Word, concluding that the weapon-salve's efficacy cannot be diabolical because the Devil only has the power to destroy, not to heal.</summary>
<keywords>weapon-salve, Robert Fludd, William Foster, divine healing, Word of God, diabolical vs divine, Psalm 107, Wisdom of Solomon, pneumatology</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes Robert Fludd’s defense against William Foster. Fludd argues that all healing—specifically the "weapon-salve" he defends—is a miracle of God, not a work of the devil. He draws on biblical authority and Renaissance philosophy to assert that the devil has no power to do good or heal.</meta>

EXPRESSION OF PART III. Folio 30

...watery or earthly, who might assist Him: For the Text says: He is the Lord of lords alone, and therefore there is no creature who helps Him, or who is able to perfect this without Him. He Himself, I say, is alone; and consequently, it is not the devil who performs miracles. But by the recognition and confession of anyone, this method of healing is miraculous, insofar as the reason for its operation exceeds the grasp of the intellect of worldly men. Therefore, with David, I shall say:

> Blessed be God, who alone performs such wonders <note>original: "Benedictus Deus, qui facit tale mirabile solus"</note>: and consequently, I may further infer (and that not without merit), cursed is the man who falsely attributes divine things to the Devil.

Therefore, I would wish that every zea<meta>catchword: lotypum</meta>lous and religious man had this inviolable emblem engraved upon his mind, so that by its virtue he might put to flight and cast out from his thoughts all such irreligious persuasions. These are the thoughts that might move him to detract even a little from the power of God, who is the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end of all things, and to falsely attribute that power to the most vile of Creatures <note>The Devil</note>. The Devil in himself is nothing, except what it has pleased God for him to be; from himself he has nothing, except what God is pleased to bestow upon him; and by himself he can perform nothing, except what God is pleased to do in him; and through him, He does it, and nothing else. Therefore, what God does not will, the Devil cannot do. Let this, then, be your emblem:

-> God is the End and Beginning of all things. <note>original Greek: "Τὸ Τέλος πάντων καὶ Ἀρχὴ ὁ Θεός"</note> <-
-> God is the end and the beginning of all. <note>original Latin: "Finis omnium ac principium est Deus"</note> <-

---

And for this reason Reuchlin <note>Johannes Reuchlin (1455–1522), a famous German scholar of the "Miraculous Word" and Hebrew Kabbalah</note> says in his book *On the Miraculous Word*: Every human miracle whose substance is found to be true and not imaginary—whether great, medium, or very small—if the prescribed sacred order is observed, must always be referred back to the glorious God, whose name is blessed forever. For He alone is the one who performs such things as we marvel at, whether by Himself, or through a delegate (not without Himself), or through a substitute acting from Him. We cannot fully know the cause of these things, either <meta>catchword: quod</meta> that they happen, or how they happen. Thus far Reuchlin.

And therefore, at the conclusion of this Treatise, I may not undeservedly apply and direct against Foster and his followers that "Woe" of Isaiah against men of the kind I mentioned at the beginning. Insofar as they presume too much on their own worldly wisdom, they hesitantly, preposterously, and with a certain blindness, ascribe the works of God to the Devil—works of goodness to the evil one; effects of light to the darkness.

### Isaiah 5: 20, 21.

Woe to those who call evil good, and good evil; who put darkness for light, and light for darkness; who put bitter for sweet, and sweet for sour bitterness. Woe to those who are wise in their own eyes, and prudent in their own sight.

<meta>catchword: Relinquo</meta> I leave these things (my hearty and learned reader), as I said before, to the most sharp, mature, and serious judgment of your consideration. I hope that the scale of equity in your judgment may be truly balanced, and not rendered unequal by corrupt and impious schism <note>partisan division or bias</note>.

-> **T H E  E N D.** <-
